# SIGKILL design doc

Native first-person shooter for Omarchy (Arch + Hyprland). You are the nixfred >_ hexagon hunting rogue daemons, zombie processes and runaway apps in a terminal arena.

## Decided
- Name: SIGKILL (Fred).
- Native installable Linux game, not web.
- Code lives at the dev machine; Larry (herdr workspace "sigkill") writes all code.
- Safety: game never modifies, deletes, executes, signals or kills anything real. Filesystem/process features read names, sizes, counts only. Demo-data mode for anything public. No Ctrl+Alt hotkeys, no focus stealing, no GPU use when not running.
- Brand: locked nixfred hexagon (icon-only, white >_) on icon, repo, README.

- Visual direction: Tron light-grid plus Matrix code rain (Fred).
- Easy internet multiplayer (room codes/invite links) is a core feature (Fred).
- Signature feature: the player's own real local AI is their in-game guide, shown live as theirs (Fred).
- Quality bar: Rockstar-level polish; Larry builds in small numbered chunks, each verified before the next (Fred).
- Full spec: PRD.md (docs/PRD.md in repo).

- (2026-10-25, Fred) Player AI = the Omarchy default agent (~/.config/omarchy/defaults/agent), called directly in non-interactive mode with tools off, never via omarchy-agent. Fallback: local model server, then scripted guide. Live GPU stats on boot and HUD. Safety rule reworded to allow only this one execution. New chunk C9b (agent adapters).
- Enemy art bar (Fred, 2026-10-25 10:23 PM ET): no cartoon monsters. Daemons are faceless, geometric, menacing: black obsidian shards with floating red terminal glyphs, angular red drone swarms, and a giant red-hot lattice core as the CPU boss. Kills derezz into glowing voxel cubes. Reference: readme-art/sigkill-epic-1.jpg and sigkill-epic-2.jpg (concept art).
- Arsenal (Fred, 2026-10-25 10:25 PM ET): many weapons, each a signal or shell command made physical (kill -9 rail rifle, fork() shotgun, grep seeker, rm -rf cannon, cron mines, ping sonar, chmod 000 freeze ray, pipe chain beam, /dev/null black hole, sudo overcharge, plus the signal set), with mods, light skins and unlocks. Full list in PRD 5.2.
- Story (Fred, 2026-10-25 10:25 PM ET): cyberpunk + Matrix + Tron campaign. A sysadmin jacked into their own machine hunts processes they never started; their AI is the only one they trust, until the trail asks who wrote the daemons. PRD 5.6, milestone M6.
- Opener (Fred, 2026-10-25 10:45 PM ET): 2-minute trapped-in-the-game cold open built from the player's real machine (fake crash in their theme, real usernames/folders/uptime, a copy of them as the first daemon, "SIGKILL sent by you to you"). Real data read-only and shown live only; Esc always works; opt-in with fake-data fallback. PRD 5.7, chunks C38-C42.
- Community prompts (Fred, 2026-10-26): build-in-public on X; accepted prompts get credited. First: Albert / @QuantaCortex's Audit Trail visor (PRD 5.5, C43). Credits roll is a live top-style process table with contributors as processes, Fred as PID 1, ending in a kill -9 derezz (PRD 5.8, C44).
- SIGSTOP fairness (producer, C18, 2026-10-27): in a match SIGSTOP never slows the host's simulation, or anyone's; nothing in a match touches the engine's time scale. The effect is self-only and local: the look and the pitched-down sound on that player's own screen, and zero spread and zero recoil only while the host says it is on. The host owns and validates every SIGSTOP meter and drops (and counts) a start the meter cannot pay for. Only solo does the world slow (0.3x), with the player compensated to 0.9x.
- Relay transport (producer, C16d, 2026-10-28): WebSocket mode added for proxy-only hosts (exe.dev); UDP mode stays as the VPS alternative; the game picks transport from the relay URL scheme.
- Fork bomb, no self-damage (producer, C19, 2026-10-28): a thrower is never hurt by its own fork bomb's blasts, solo or in a match; it keeps it fun.
- Fork bomb, host-owned chain (producer, C19, 2026-10-28): in a match the host owns the whole chain. A client sends only its throw (origin and aim), which the host validates against its view of the player's position and aim and its count of the bombs; the host simulates every bomblet and judges all damage; clients get one seed per throw to draw the same scatter, and the host's blast events, and never decide damage.
- Browser version (Fred, 2026-10-28 12:46 AM ET): parked for release 2.0, not 1.0. Plan: a Godot web export (WebGL renderer, shader fallbacks) using the WebSocket relay for multiplayer, demo-data maps only (no filesystem, processes, local announcer or voice in a browser). Native game ships first.
- Light-trail mode (producer, 2026-10-28): an optional match mode, off by default, turned on by the host in the Esc menu's settings (Multiplayer, "light trails") or with the launch flag `-- --light-trails`; the host's setting is the match's and joiners get it from the host. Every player's movement on the ground lays thin neon wall segments behind them in that player's own colour from the theme palette (no new art assets: simple meshes and materials); airborne lays none. Segments live a few seconds, then fade and free, with a per-player cap. Touching any light wall, anyone's, your own older trail included, kills you with the existing derezz death and credits the trail's owner (a self-kill if your own); your own newest segments get a short grace. A death or respawn clears that player's trail; leaving the match frees all of it. In a match the host is authoritative for trail kills, in the netcode's existing pattern; joiners see the same walls; the relay protocol is unchanged. Numbers (resources/light_trail.tres): a segment every 0.5 m of ground travel, 1.2 m tall, 0.08 m thick, standing 6 s with a 0.75 s fade, 64 segments a player, 0.4 s of grace on your own (your newest never touches you), the ground within 0.15 m of the feet, a new wall past a 2.5 m gap.
- Code-rain vision (producer, 2026-10-28): an ability, not a weapon. Hold V (a new rebindable action, "code_rain_vision", in the settings' key list; never a Ctrl+Alt binding; keyboard and mouse only for now); releasing it or an empty meter turns it off. Its own meter, separate from SIGSTOP's: a full meter lasts about 3 s of use, refills fully in about 12 s after about 1 s of rest, and needs at least about 25% to start; every number in resources/code_rain_vision.tres. While on, every enemy body within about 40 m (the target dummies alone; the other players in a match) is drawn as a green glyph silhouette that shows through walls, built from the existing code rain look (no new art assets, simple materials and shaders only), and the world dims and tints toward green on your screen only; your weapons fire as usual. Purely local and visual: in a match it draws only positions the client already has; no new network messages, protocol stays 6, the host's authority and the relay untouched, and nothing about your vision is sent to anyone. It respects the comfort settings (a calmer tint, no flashing), and the HUD shows its meter next to SIGSTOP's. A death or respawn turns it off and leaves the meter as it was; leaving a match turns it off. Numbers picked: meter 3 s, refill 12 s after 1 s of rest, start 25%, range 40 m, tint (0.45, 0.8, 0.5) at strength 1 or 0.5 calm, 0.2 s ease, silhouettes 0.6 m by 1.9 m with glow 3, the nearest 16 at most.
- Root vs user and sudo overcharge (producer, 2026-10-28): every life starts as user; the HUD shows a small prompt badge, "user$" normally and "root#" while overcharged, in the theme's warning red for root. About 3 kills in one life without dying (target dummy derezzes alone; kills credited to you in a match, light-trail wall kills included) earn one sudo charge; at most 1 held; dying resets the streak and drops an unused charge (you respawn as user). Press Z (a new rebindable action, "sudo_overcharge", in the settings key list; never a Ctrl+Alt binding): the HUD flashes a purely cosmetic "[sudo] password for user: ********" line, then root# for about 8 s. While root the weapon in hand (and any you swap to) does about 1.5x damage and uses no ammo; the rail's ult meter is not filled faster and its shot is not doubled (it just gets the 1.5x); a clear overcharge look on the weapon and muzzle (simple tint and glow, no new art); SIGSTOP and code-rain vision unchanged. Root ends at the timer, on death, on respawn and on leaving a match. All numbers (kills needed 3, duration 8 s, damage multiplier 1.5, charges held at most 1) in resources/sudo_overcharge.tres. Alone all local; in a match host-owned: the host counts kills, grants the charge, accepts or refuses activation, applies the 1.5x in its own shot judging and tells clients who is root so everyone sees the badge and glow; the minimum new messages, protocol 7, an old-protocol peer gets the existing version-mismatch refusal; clients never apply the multiplier themselves; the relay untouched beyond carrying the new messages. Fiction only: the game never runs, calls or mentions a real sudo command or reads anything about the real user; "user" is a fixed word, not the player's username.
- Spectate as top (producer, 2026-10-28): while you are dead in a match (from your derezz until your respawn, the existing 3 s respawn delay) your HUD shows a full-width top-style table of the match, drawn in the Omarchy theme colours, monospace, readable at 960x540; it goes away the moment you respawn, and the dead player's camera stays as it is (this chunk is the table, not a new camera). Hold Tab (a new rebindable action, "show_top", in the settings key list; never a Ctrl+Alt binding) to show the same table any time, alive or dead, alone or in a match; alone the rows are you plus the target dummies as daemon processes (COMMAND "dummy-1" and on, state Z while derezzing or respawning). Header, top-style: "top - <round clock> up <time since match start>, <N> users, load average: a, b, c", the load being the match's kills in the last 10, 30 and 60 seconds of play, then "Tasks: <total> total, <r> running, <s> stopped, <z> zombie". Columns: PID (a stable game number per player for the match, by join order from 100; game data, not a real PID), USER ("user" or "root" from C22's root state; fixed words, never the real username), S (R alive, Z dead, T while that player's SIGSTOP is on if the viewer's game knows it, else R), %CPU (that player's share of the damage dealt this round, 0.0 to 100.0), RES (health), TIME+ (time alive this life, m:ss.hh), K (kills), D (deaths), COMMAND (the player's in-game name); sorted by K descending, then %CPU descending, like top; your own row highlighted. It refreshes once a second, like top, not every frame. All numbers (the 1.0 s refresh, the 10/30/60 load windows, the rows shown at most, PID base 100) in resources/spectate_top.tres. Network: built from what every peer already receives where it can; only a column that truly needs host data gets one minimal host-owned table message at no more than 1 Hz, protocol 8, an old-protocol peer getting the existing version-mismatch refusal (with no message the protocol stays 7); the host's authority and the relay untouched beyond carrying it; a client never invents another player's numbers. During the round break the summary has priority: the table hides or keeps clear of it, so the two never overlap at 960x540. Fiction only: the table never reads, lists or mentions real processes, PIDs, users, hostnames or paths; no OS.get_process_id, /proc, ps, top, OS.execute or OS.create_process in the new code.
- fork() shotgun (producer, 2026-10-28): weapon slot 5 (key 5, and in the Q last-weapon swap and the weapon cycle like the others). Each shot fires a spread of pellets that are short-lived fast projectiles, not instant hitscan, so the split is real and visible: each parent pellet travels a fork distance and is then replaced by two child pellets angled apart by a fork angle in the plane of the spread, flying on at the same speed; one fork generation normally (6 pellets become 12). A parent that hits something before its fork distance does full pellet damage and never forks; each child does a fraction of its parent's damage (0.6 to start), so point-blank does the most total damage and range spreads it out. Pellets have a longest range and then vanish, and they stop at walls. sudo overcharge (C22) on this weapon: the children fork once more (two generations, up to 24), using the overcharge rules already in place, and no other overcharge effect. Fire rate, magazine and E reload like the other shotguns, slower than SIGTERM; its own light colour from the theme palette with thin glowing pellet trails, and a fork shows as a small flash; the kill feed and the callouts follow the existing pattern ("fork()"). Every number (pellet count, speed, fork distance, fork angle, child damage fraction, generations, overcharge generations, the longest range, fire interval, magazine, reload time, spread) in resources/fork_shotgun.tres, in the existing weapon tuning style, no magic numbers in code. Network: the host stays authoritative for hits and damage like the other weapons, reusing the existing shot and hit messages so the protocol stays 8 if it can (a truly needed new message would mean protocol 9, old peers refused with the existing version mismatch); the pellet simulation is deterministic from the shot's origin, direction and seed, so every peer draws the same forks, and a client never decides damage; the relay untouched beyond carrying traffic. Fiction only: the weapon never calls fork(), OS.execute or OS.create_process, reads /proc or touches anything real.
- grep seeker (producer, 2026-10-28): weapon slot 6 (key 6, and in the Q last-weapon swap and the weapon cycle like the others), PRD 5.2's seeker smart gun on the C16 framework. Marking: while grep is in hand and the existing aim action is held (or toggled, per the C16b setting), a target (a dummy alone, another player in a match) inside a narrow lock cone round the crosshair, within lock range and with line of sight, builds a lock over a lock time and is then marked; up to a cap of marks at once (3). A mark drops when its target leaves line of sight or range for longer than a grace time, derezzes, or the weapon is swapped. Marked targets show a thin bracket in grep's own theme colour with a small "match" tag, the brackets tightening as a lock builds; the HUD shows "grep -c: N". No new key bindings. Firing: one seeker dart per mark (spread over the marks), or one straight dart with no mark; darts are fast projectiles with a limited turn rate (they home, and can miss a target that breaks hard or ducks behind cover), stop at walls, vanish at their range; a shot spends the marks; damage per dart, fire interval, magazine and E reload like the other weapons, mid-range power, weaker a hit than the cannon, better at tracking. sudo overcharge (C22): its general rules hold (1.5 times the damage, no ammo spent, host-judged), plus grep's own: locks build twice as fast and the cap on marks rises to 5; nothing else. Every number in resources/grep_seeker.tres, no magic numbers in code. Look: its own light colour (not the cannon's, SIGTERM's, the rail's, the fork bomb's or fork()'s), thin glowing dart trails; kill feed and callouts "grep". Network: the host stays authoritative; the shot intent carries the shooter's claimed mark target ids, the host re-checks each claim (range, line of sight, cone with a small tolerance, target alive) and drops bad ones, simulates the darts deterministically from origin, aim, seed and the believed targets and judges them with the existing combat path (root included); existing messages reused, protocol 8 if at all possible, a truly needed change means protocol 9 and the existing version refusal; joiners draw the same darts and never decide damage; the relay untouched. Fiction only: nothing runs grep, OS.execute or OS.create_process, reads /proc, files or anything real. The strip must fit six slots at 960x540. As built, three readings of ours; the producer confirmed the first and the third after the build (2026-10-28), the second stands as ours: "line of sight" for keeping a mark means in sight, within a sight cone of the crosshair (40 degrees, a target behind you is out of sight) with nothing of the level between, and the host's "cone with a small tolerance" is that sight cone plus 4 degrees (the narrow 6 degree lock cone at the shot would refuse any mark but the one under the crosshair); the cannon has no headshot bonus, so grep's 18 a dart is set under the cannon's 25 a hit; and protocol 9, since the intent must carry the claims and the seed message the believed marks, two existing messages gaining a field (no new message), and a protocol 8 peer still connecting would stall a joiner's ammo count on its first grep shot.
- rm -rf cannon (producer, 2026-10-28): weapon slot 7 (key 7, and in the Q last-weapon swap and the weapon cycle like the others), PRD 5.2's slow, devastating cannon with friendly-fire risk, on the C16 framework. Confirm wind-up: fire starts a short wind-up (the "are you sure" beat) before the shot leaves, with a small "rm: remove? [y]" prompt on the HUD filling as it charges; letting go early does not cancel it, the shot goes when it ends; a swap, a death or a respawn during it cancels it and spends no ammo; while it winds up and just after the shot the shooter moves slower (a move speed factor). No new key bindings. Shot: one slow, heavy glowing slug flying straight, clearly slower than every other projectile, detonating on a wall, the floor, any target or at its longest range. Blast: area damage falling off from the centre to the edge of its radius, enough at the centre to derezz a full-health player and only a scratch at the edge; level geometry between blast and target blocks it. Friendly fire: the blast hurts everything in its radius, the shooter included at a tuned fraction (point-blank is a real mistake); any team or co-op grouping is ignored, and with none yet it is simply everyone; a self-kill reads as the shooter removing themself ("rm -rf: <name> removed themself"), other kills "rm -rf". Ammo: magazine 1, a small reserve, a long E reload, a long fire interval. sudo (C22): its general rules (1.5x damage, no ammo spent, host-judged) plus "--no-preserve-root": a larger blast radius and a shorter wind-up; self-damage still applies; nothing else. Every number in resources/rm_rf_cannon.tres. Look: its own palette colour, a heavy glowing slug with a thick trail, a bright expanding blast ring; feed and callouts "rm -rf". Network: the host stays authoritative; the usual intent (sent when the wind-up ends); the host runs the slug and blast and judges every damage and kill with the existing combat path (root and self-damage included); joiners draw them and never decide damage; existing messages, protocol 9 if possible; the relay untouched. Fiction only, a hard rule: nothing runs rm, OS.execute, OS.create_process, DirAccess.remove, OS.move_to_trash or touches files, /proc or the system, with a test that greps for them. Seven slots at 960x540 clear of the guide panel. Ours as built (the brief's "decide toward slow, devastating, readable, fair"): ember orange (#ff6a1a) as its colour, between hostile amber and red but neither, the blast's danger in it; the wind-up is the rail's charge (so the Esc menu cancels it too, as it does the rail's) and its prompt types out on the strip's third line, where the rail's charge shows; no slide starts while the weight is on; alone the player has no health (as since C14), so the blast only finds the daemons and the self-damage is a match's; each blast of the shooter's is called out "rm -rf" at once, as the fork bomb's first blast is; a blast on a surface goes off 5 cm back from it, so the surface shields what is behind; a joiner's slug passes bodies, holds at a wall or its range and goes off only on the host's word (fork bomb's _blast), waiting 0.5 s for it; the slot line at the small lines' 12 px; the numbers (0.6 s and 0.3 s, half pace for 0.8 s, 9 m/s, 45 m, 6 m and 9 m, 140 to 8, half to the shooter, 1 round, 3 in reserve, 3.2 s, 2.5 s).
- cron mines (producer, 2026-10-28): weapon slot 8 (key 8, and in the Q last-weapon swap and the weapon cycle like the others), PRD 5.2's timed mines, on the C16 framework and C19/C26's blast path. A mine is a scheduled job: fire places one on the surface aimed at (floor or wall) within a short placement range, at the feet with nothing in reach, and it sticks there. After a short arm delay it runs on a fixed schedule, an area blast every interval, a set number of runs (3), then the job ends and it fades; each run is clearly telegraphed (a closing countdown ring and a brief brighten), and tiny cron-style text over it shows its schedule and runs left. Each run is an area blast with centre-to-edge falloff and cover blocking, through the existing blast judge, moderate (a full-health player at the centre survives one run and not all of them). The owner's own mines never hurt the owner (the opposite of rm -rf: a zoning tool); every other player in reach is hurt, teams ignored; alone they hit daemons; feed and callouts "cron". Each player has at most a set number of active mines (3): one more removes the oldest, noted briefly on the HUD, which shows the active count ("crontab 2/3") on the strip while weapon 8 is in hand. Mines keep running if the owner dies or respawns and still credit the owner; they go when the owner leaves and when a round resets. Any other player can shoot a mine with any weapon to remove it ("crontab -r" in the feed), host-judged. Ammo: magazine 3, a modest reserve, a medium E reload, a short placement interval. sudo (C22): the general rules (1.5x, no ammo spent, host-judged) plus "root crontab", one extra run for a mine placed as root; nothing else. Every number in resources/cron_mines.tres. Look: its own palette colour, a small glowing disc or hex stud with a ring closing before each run and a blast ring per run. Network: host-authoritative; the usual intent; the host places, schedules and runs every mine and judges every damage, kill and removal with the existing combat path (root included); joiners draw and never decide damage or schedules; a mid-match joiner sees mines already placed if that falls out naturally; existing messages, protocol 9 if possible; the relay untouched. Fiction only, a hard rule: "cron" is a name and HUD text, nothing touches the real crontab, timers, at, files, /proc, the clock or the system, with a test that greps for such calls (and crontab/systemctl as calls) and tells HUD strings apart. Eight slots at 960x540 clear of the guide panel, the smallest change if they do not fit (two rows wait for a ninth weapon). Ours as built (the brief's "readable zoning, fair counterplay, clearly telegraphed"): rose pink (#ff5fb0), between the fork bomb's magenta and hostile red but neither; the first run comes as the arm delay ends (the arming is its telegraph), then one every interval; the schedule text is "*/2s x3", its xN the runs left counting down; a mine sits 5 cm off its face; its hit volume is on its own physics layer, so it never stops anyone's shot or step and only the host's shot judging sees it; counterplay is every weapon's (a hitscan ray removes the first mine it passes, the rail every one on its line, a pellet, dart or slug that meets one, a blast that reaches one, another player's cron run included), never a shooter's own, and reads "crontab -r: <shooter> removed <owner>'s job"; rounds are each screen's own since C12, so a new round clears mines alone only, in a match they end with their job, the crontab, a shot or their owner leaving; the strip's third line shows the crontab when nothing more urgent (a reload, the fiction line, an empty magazine) holds it, and the note is "crontab full: oldest out", short enough for 960x540; the first run of each of the player's mines is called out "cron" at the usual pace; the slot line one point smaller again (11 px) for eight slots; a mid-match joiner is sent every running mine; on the wire a mine's seed is its runs left (negative once armed), a run's word its runs left after it, a removal's a negative code with the shooter's id; the numbers (4 m, 1 s, every 2 s, 3 runs and 1 more as root, 3 jobs, 0.35 m to hit, 0.6 s fade, 0.3 s brighten, 55 to 6 over 3 m, 3 mines and 6, 2.4 s, 0.5 s).
- Domain (Fred, 2026-10-28 8:45 AM ET): playsigkill.com. Site brief with demo-data screenshots goes to Website Builder Bot; buying the domain and taking the site live still need Fred's yes.
- Art pass (producer, 2026-10-28 9:05 AM ET): runs right after C27 and before C28, so playsigkill.com launches with screenshots worth showing. Original procedural/shader art only, no paid or ripped assets.
- A1 weapon viewmodels (producer brief 2026-10-28; decided as built, ours, 2026-10-28): every weapon is held as its own model, built once in code from boxes, prisms, tubes and a ">" glyph through a SurfaceTool kit (scripts/viewmodels/viewmodel.gd), one script per weapon, merged into one mesh with a surface per material, faceted low poly (4632 triangles for all eight), never rebuilt while held; no CSG and no scene files, so nothing is computed at load beyond the merge; each WeaponDef names its model in a new visual field, viewmodel_model (a script; none gets the old glowing box, WeaponDef.new() the terminal cannon's); the trim is the model node's own mesh with the glow as its material_override, so the player's glow, charge glow and root's red work as before and reach every part; the body is one original shader (shaders/viewmodel_metal.gdshader: model-space panel lines or hex cells, a faint neon rim) over palette colours GUNMETAL #0c1014, GUNMETAL_LIGHT #1c232a and POLYMER #060708, the hot parts the weapon's beam colour; the silhouettes: the terminal cannon a compact carbine with a two-fin brake and a ">_" prompt, SIGTERM an over-under pump with violet rings and "please exit" chevrons, the rail two rails round a lit core with four coils and a long scope, the fork bomb a hex cage round a turning drum of six lit pods held turned 38 degrees toward the eye and 14 up, fork() one barrel forking into two prongs 13 degrees apart each side, grep a fat scope with lit lenses and a three-dart pod, rm -rf a fat octagonal housing with vent fins and hazard stripes, cron a dispenser with the next hex mine loaded and a clock whose hand goes round once a run interval; side glyphs on the left face, the one toward the eye; the shot still leaves from the eyes (gameplay and the wire untouched), the Muzzle marker only moves where beams and the muzzle flash start, projectiles are still drawn from the eyes, and the rm -rf slug is not drawn for its first 0.75 m (it filled the view); the held motion in ArsenalTuning's new Viewmodel motion group: a lag of 0.025 s of the turn's rate capped at 5 degrees settling at 9 a second, a stride of 8 by 6 mm and 1.5 degrees of roll (off with head bob), a fall's trail of 2.5 mm per m/s capped at 2 cm, a lift of 0.35 of the kick back, a 22 degree tip on the raise, 0.35 of all of it aimed; the kick by each weapon's own visual fields, SIGTERM's made light (0.06 m, 6 degrees, settling at 14; was 0.14, 14, 9), the rail's (0.22 m, 10) and rm -rf's (0.24 m, 20) left heavy; every viewmodel_offset moved right and low for the 80 degree view (cannon 0.24, -0.22, -0.5; SIGTERM 0.24, -0.24, -0.48; rail 0.22, -0.22, -0.55; fork bomb 0.2, -0.2, -0.38; fork() 0.24, -0.24, -0.48; grep 0.23, -0.24, -0.48; rm -rf 0.25, -0.27, -0.55; cron 0.24, -0.25, -0.46); a charge piece lights from the weapon's own glow (0.35 at rest, 2.5 more full), the rail's coils in order, rm -rf's core and vents together; the eye-side scope lenses trim, not hot; the fork bomb's drum a quarter turn a second, grep's eye-side lens breathing at 0.8 Hz, cron's stud beating once a run interval; muzzle_offset and viewmodel_size are the placeholder box's only now; the captures: tools/capture_shots.gd driven by tools/capture_shots.sh on a private Xvnc display (:97, no TCP, its VNC side on a 0600 unix socket, a fresh cookie as its only way in), Godot on X11 only with an empty private runtime folder so it can never reach the desktop's Wayland, sound off, user data in a work folder, the demo guide link parked as the tests park theirs, 1920x1080 stills, mid-shot 3 frames after a shot, mid-charge at 80% of a charge, the rail's meter filled for its stills, cron's placed looking 24 degrees down, the wide view from (4, 0.95, 4) on DummyC between the pillars 7 frames after the rail's shot; the after stills from the last code commit (the docs commit that adds them cannot hold its own), the before stills from 5ee88b1 with the same tool copied in; the stills in docs/media as lossless PNG; scripts/play.sh imports when .godot/global_script_class_cache.cfg is missing, then runs godot --path with any options; the viewmodel check as step 32.
- A2 enemy art (producer brief 2026-10-28; decided as built, ours, 2026-10-28): the game's only enemy today is the target dummy, four of them tagged rogue.d, zombie, <defunct> and runaway; no swarm or boss exists yet (they come with M5's process enemies), so A2 dresses the dummies by the PRD's enemy words: rogue.d a rogue daemon (a red-hot crystal core in a twisted crown of five obsidian blades and seven leaning shards, on three spikes meeting in a point, hovering), zombie and <defunct> a zombie process (a defunct process is a zombie: an obsidian obelisk broken into six chunks knocked off true, grinding two ways round an ember guttering hostile amber, its tip floating tilted, debris drifting; it stands), runaway the art bar's angular drone swarm (thirteen obsidian darts with blade fins and glowing tails in three tilted rings round a small hot core); no boss art, since nothing in the game is a boss and a boss in a screenshot would be fiction; each target names its art in a new visual field, `art` (a script; the rogue daemon is the dummy's default, arena.tscn sets the other three); the art is built under the dummy's Body, so a derezz hides it and a respawn grows it back with no new state; the Body's hex prism stays as the volume (size, centre, head zone, the derezz's shape) and is drawn by no camera (layers 0); the shape kit moved out of A1's Viewmodel into scripts/art/shape_kit.gd (A1's meshes byte-identical) and gained spikes and edged buckets; daemon shells draw their true edges as thin red lines from per-vertex barycentrics with the triangulation's inner edges out of reach (a flat-shaded fresnel rim lit whole faces red); the zombie's chunks are knocked off true so no crack reads as eyes; cores hostile red for the rogue daemon and the swarm, hostile amber for the zombie, guttering; OBSIDIAN #030405 and the glitch split's GLITCH_SPLIT #4de1ff in the palette; the glyphs are procedural 4x6 bit grids like the code rain's, no font; the glitch is bands jumping sideways with a red and blue split of the scanlines a few ticks a second, far more while hit or rematerialising; every daemon of every type shares one obsidian, one core and one glyph material, per-daemon values as instance uniforms, so no daemon compiles a shader of its own; each daemon's clock is its own, advanced by the process delta at the game's pace, so solo SIGSTOP slows its turning, flicker and glitch with the world (in a match nothing touches the time scale, as decided in C18); the grep lock is a cobalt sweep, read by polling a `grep_lock` group GrepLock now joins, every 0.1 s; the hit flash rides the dummy's existing non-lethal pulse; the rematerialise glitch runs from full down to none as it grows; the derezz upgraded in place, not replaced: edge-lit voxels whose faces fall away cell by cell as they fade, and a column of light and a spreading ground ring for 0.35 s, children of the effect, every number and the timing kept; the players' own death is the same effect, its flash red as its voxels already were; the record (rogue daemon 136 triangles in 4 draw calls, zombie 126 in 5, swarm 340 in 8, plus one shadow draw per obsidian layer per shadowing light; a derezz adds 2 draws for 0.35 s); the capture tool's a2 set (each kind up close, a swarm and a zombie in one view, a derezz 7 frames after the shot, grep locked on the defunct zombie between the pillars); after stills from the last code commit, before stills from a git archive of 15e777d; the enemy art check as step 33, the hit volume checked as literal text against 15e777d.
- A3 arena (producer brief 2026-10-28; decided as built, ours, 2026-10-28): visual only, and no new collision anywhere: rather than prove new collision safe, nothing of the dressing collides, everything a player can walk into stands within 0.3 m of a block (under a player's 0.4 m radius, so the eyes never enter it), nothing hangs over a top a player can stand on lower than a jump reaches (1.8 m tall plus a 1.22 m jump), and a block under 2.5 m tall gets a lip under its edge, never a crown above it; the test proves the collision and 20 spawn-to-daemon sight lines byte for byte against b38a900. Data-driven: one ArenaArt node (scripts/arena/arena_art.gd) dresses every grey block by its own size and kind (kick plate, bevelled crown or lip, corner guards, ribs every 2.6 m, waist rail, inset frames on faces 6 m wide or more that look into the arena, teal strips low, mint high) and hangs props and signs on blocks by name; built once, kept in a static cache keyed by the layout, so an R reload builds nothing. "Varied heights" and "raised platforms" where no one walks: a stepped plinth under the arena (three tiers falling into the fog on a column), parapets of varied heights on the outer walls (from a hash of the wall and place), corner towers and masts; the raised platform's "stepped edges" are two shallow visual steps (0.27 and 0.135 m out), not steps a player climbs. The lair (the rogue daemon's room, an AABB export on ArenaArt) gets hostile red strips. Signs: an original 5 by 7 pixel terminal font written as bit rows in scripts/arena/arena_font.gd (upper case, digits, a lower-case x for hex, the symbols used), not the system font: no font dependency, the same triangles on every machine, a terminal look; texts invented flavour (RING 0, KILL -9, EXIT 137, PID 1, TTY0, SIGKILL, ARENA 01, /DEV/NULL, CORE DUMPED, SIGTERM, SIGSTOP, SUDO !!, 01, 02, 0x1F, TMP, >>>, /PROC/ARENA). Holograms: one MultiMesh turning and bobbing in the vertex stage. Skyline (scripts/arena/arena_skyline.gd): seeded (0x51C1), 72 towers 110 to 430 m out rising 0.14 to 0.42 m per metre away so they clear the walls seen from the floor, 4 giant data towers 520 to 640 m out with 1200 m beams, an orbital ring (radius 180 m, 150 m up, tilted 14 and 6 degrees, 0.6 degrees a second), two drifting groups (0.35 and -0.22 degrees a second); the skyline turns at the game's pace (it slows with SIGSTOP), while the arena's shader ambience (strip waves, screens, flow, holograms, beacons) runs on TIME like the code rain. Fog: a cheap depth fog, not volumetric (30 to 600 m, curve 0.6, density 0.85; height fog below -4 m at 0.035; sky affect 0.5; FOG #031214); the sky a shader without TIME, so its radiance (32) is worked out once. Floor: floor_grid.gdshader reworked into dark deck plates (Fred's "flat cyan grid floor"): the 1 m grid down from 1.0 to 0.05, 2 m plates with seams, insets, vents and tone; the 8 m light strips kept. Walls: the code rain shader gets a faint strip wash (0.04); no new lights (lighting is A4). Materials: twelve shared arena materials from five spatial shaders, plus the sky, made once in palette.gd, every colour and energy there. Budgets: dressing 32,000 triangles and 16 draw calls, skyline 8,000 and 12, the whole arena with its blocks 42,000 and 68 (a draw call a surface, one more for each that throws a shadow). The quality preset is untouched (A4). Captures: the overview and the skyline from free cameras with the player and HUD hidden, the corridor, the lair's front and the gameplay view in play; before stills from b38a900 with A3's capture tool laid over it. tests/rail_test.gd's derezz count counts derezz effects by their group (every MultiMesh counted made it always true since A2's glyphs). The README's list of single-test commands gained the viewmodel and enemy art tests it was missing.
- A2b enemy redo (producer, 2026-10-28 12:58 PM ET, after Fred's 'boxes, yawn' feedback on A2): every enemy articulated and procedurally animated (rogue.d crystal spider/mantis, zombie derezzed limping wireframe with ghost copies, runaway boids swarm), hit reactions, idle motion, heavier glitch shader, 1.5-2.5x visual scale, shard death; visual only, hitboxes and gameplay numbers unchanged; check step 35; A4 becomes step 36.
- A2b enemy redo, as built (ours, 2026-10-28): every creature is rigid crystal parts posed each frame, not a skinned mesh: each part one instance of a shared unit shape (a six-sided crystal shard, an octagonal cut gem, a voxel, a trail sliver, made once from the shape kit) in one of three looks (shell, core, trail), one MultiMesh per shape and look, moved by transforms only; nothing made per frame. rogue.d: six legs by two-bone IK with planted feet (a foot lifts when its home is 0.24 m off and the other tripod is planted, or 2.2 times that at once), an alternating tripod, a seeded skittering loop; the dummies never attack, so 'rears up before it attacks' is a threat display every 6 s and at once on a hit (rear, tremble, lunge and slash at nothing); its back shards flex and one at a time breaks and re-forms. The zombie is a pure function of its clock (gait, twitches, flicker, shed voxels), so each ghost is the same function 0.3, 0.6 and 0.9 s earlier, set 0.42 m further back along its way; it hunches (its pelvis walks 0.26 m behind its loop) so its head stays over the hit volume's head zone; its edges amber, its wire bones and ghosts edges only, ghosts alternating the split's cyan and the edge colour. The swarm: 36 boids (every pair each frame), each on its own orbit tilted off the swarm's precessing axis, dives at the ground, light trails from two history points, a scatter that may outrun the top speed for 0.6 s; stepped with the frame's game delta, so seeded per daemon but not frame-rate independent (the rogue's feet the same; the zombie is exact). Every type: a 0.16 m spring recoil away from the shot and ten glowing shards sprayed from where it struck, then its own answer (the rogue rears, the swarm scatters). Death: the creature's own parts burst under the derezz effect (top level, so it goes with the derezz; DerezzFx and its timing untouched), shells solid, glitching at full, stepping the shared shader clock. Shaders: one shader global daemon_clock (project.godot shader globals) stepped once a frame by whichever daemon or burst processes first, offset per daemon by its seed; instance uniforms pinned in every daemon shader (hit 0, locked 1, unstable 2, seed 3, core_color 4, gutter 5, edge_color 6); the glitch shared in shaders/daemon_glitch.gdshaderinc (jumping bands, a scanline tear along the camera's right, a part snapping for a tick); the RGB split a next pass on the shell material (one roll per part, never per vertex, pulled 0.12 m toward the camera so its own shell does not hide it). Scale: the diagonal of the box round all a creature draws over a second, against A2's at 1afcdb7; 1.9 to 2.3 times. The creatures reach past the hit volume (legs, the swarm, ghosts); the zombie's head stays inside its height. Budgets per type, a draw call a set plus its split pass plus a shadow: rogue 2,000 triangles and 10 draw calls, zombie 3,600 and 10, swarm 5,600 and 8. Name tags float per type (2.85, 2.75 and 2.6 m) from target_dummy.gd, its scene untouched. Tests: the A2 enemy art test (step 33) and the code rain test's daemon check moved onto the rig, same intent; the new check is step 35, A4 step 36. Captures: heroes and strips from free cameras, a strip three stills cut 1120 by 900 side by side, the rogue's waiting for its rear; the view in play from (-1.5, 18.5), the one spot where all three kinds are in frame (the lair's only window from the south is its door, so the rogue is small); before stills from 1afcdb7 with the same tool; the A2 stills leave the README but stay in docs/media (deleting them waits on Fred).
- Codex is back as SIGKILL's bug hunter, called by Larry on each chunk's diff (Fred, 2026-10-28 4:22 PM ET; replaces the 2026-10-26 no-Codex rule).
- Codex bug hunts are off for now (Fred, 2026-10-28 8:04 PM ET; replaces the 4:22 PM Codex-back rule until Fred turns it back on).
- The repository is public (Fred, 2026-10-28 9:50 PM ET, after a clean secret scan; early commits naming the dev machine were accepted as-is). MIT license (Fred, 2026-10-28 9:57 PM ET); still pre-alpha.
- A5 monster redo (Fred, 2026-10-28 9:50 PM ET: "All the enemies look the same, have the same color... I want REALLY cool monsters"): every daemon gets a solid dark armored body with light only in seams and weak points (Tron Legacy style, not glowing wireframe), its own color and silhouette, still faceless and geometric. rogue.d: black obsidian mantis-scorpion, crimson seams, segmented carapace, scythe arms, one visor slit, rears tall before the lunge. zombie: hulking amber process, armor on one half and voxels peeling off the other, a cannon arm that charges its bolt, a limp. runaway: swarm of solid hot-magenta hex drones with blade wings orbiting a bigger core drone; killing the core scatters the swarm. Queued right after C28.
- No clone daemons (Fred, 2026-10-28 9:51 PM ET: "dont make all the monsters of the same type be exact copies that gets boring"): A5 builds each daemon procedurally from a per-spawn seed, so every instance of a type differs in size (within a hitbox-safe range), armor plate layout, seam pattern, stance, gait timing and battle damage (chipped plates, flickering seams); a rare elite variant per type is bigger with extra parts. Type color and silhouette stay readable so the player still knows what it is. Demo/test seeds are fixed so captures and tests stay deterministic.
- ICE and ICEbreakers (Fred, 2026-10-28 10:21 PM ET, "add this to the mix"; community prompt by Tim Provencio, @TimProvencio, public reply on X, credited as the second community feature after @QuantaCortex): ICE (William Gibson's intrusion countermeasure electronics) is a new enemy class, separate from daemons: the system defending itself against the player as intruder. Three tiers: white ICE (barriers that seal corridors, jam weapons, corrupt the HUD), hunter ICE (tracks the player and adapts by countering their most-used weapon), Black ICE (rare and frightening, not a bigger health bar: lights die, music drops, the AI guide link is cut, normal weapons cannot kill it; touching it flatlines you, out for the rest of the round in a match, run over in solo). ICEbreakers are one-use programs carried beside weapons: brute (cracks ICE), exploit (hits weak seams, ties into A5 seam weak points), cloak (hides you), decoy (sacrifice it to escape). The AI guide recommends which breaker to load from round numbers only, with the scripted guide as fallback. Later, with process enemies (1.x), ICE classes can take their look from the names of security software running on the host: names only, read-only, demo data for anything public. Everything is simulated; nothing real is touched. Chunks I1 (white and hunter ICE), I2 (ICEbreakers and AI pick), I3 (Black ICE), queued after A5 and before C29.
- A4 lighting, post and particles (producer, 2026-10-28, after A2b passed): every frame finished and with colour contrast in the Tron/Matrix direction: hostile reds and magentas on daemons and danger, warm amber/orange on signage, pickups or key lights; tuned glow with no blown-out mush, a chosen tonemapper (reason recorded), SSAO and SSR only where cheap, a subtle grade, chromatic aberration and CRT scanlines as settings; GPU particles (muzzle flash per weapon family, impact sparks and derezz bits, projectile trails), shared materials, capped counts, no per-frame allocation; a graphics quality preset (low, medium, high, plus individual toggles), low clearly cheaper, saved like the other settings, medium smooth on a 4050-class GPU; fold-ins if cheap: a fixed viewmodel projection, projectiles drawn from the muzzle, SIGSTOP slowing the strips' wave; check step 36; visual and settings only: no gameplay number, hitbox, scripts/net or protocol change; Codex reviews the chunk's diff read-only, Larry writes every fix.
- A4 as built (ours, 2026-10-28): the tonemapper is AgX (contrast 1.35, white 10, exposure 1.05): very bright saturated neon rolls off to white without ACES's hue skew, so hot red never drifts orange and teal never goes flat cyan-white, and the contrast keeps the dark world deep. The glow mixes by Screen over a 0.9 HDR threshold (only the neon glows, never a dark face) with a luminance cap of 5 (a kill flash or a swarm can no longer spread into a white blot), levels 2 to 6 weighted from a tight core to a wide halo. SSAO (short and light) and SSR (40 steps) are the high preset's only; the floor's deck plates are gloss (roughness 0.18, seams and vents matt) so SSR has the neon to reflect. The grade: contrast 1.06, saturation 1.1 and a one-row per-channel curve that lifts blue in the shadows and warms the highlights, always on (it costs nothing). Chromatic aberration (3 px at a corner, none at the middle) and CRT scanlines (a 3 px period, a faint aperture grille, a vignette) are off by default and in no preset, taste not cost, drawn on one full-screen layer under the HUD and hidden while both are off; neither warps the picture, so the crosshair stays true. Presets: low drops bloom, the sun's shadows, the code rain, SSAO, SSR and the skyline's orbital ring and drifting slabs, a quarter of each effect's particles and no trails; medium (the 4050-class target) keeps glow, rain and the full skyline with half the particles and no shadows, SSAO or SSR; high has everything. Each preset key is a toggle of its own; one changed by hand makes the preset read custom (never picked from the screen); a settings file from before A4 gives each missing toggle its preset's value. Colour: the big wall signs in warm sodium (#ff8f26, a new palette colour for signs and their lamps only) each with a warm lamp before it (7, no shadows), two red danger lights pulsing in the lair, and a daemon's glitch split in hostile magenta (#ff2bd6, was cyan), which the derezz bits share. Particles: one node of fixed pools (12 spark, 8 derezz, 4 blast, 12 trail, 2 heavy trail emitters; 1,344 particles at the most), every kind one process material and one mesh, every colour one draw material; an effect takes the next emitter round its pool and restarts it, so the pools are the caps and nothing is made per shot (every colour an effect can take has its draw material made with the pools); a projectile's trail borrows a follower while it flies (none free: none). The particle level sets every emitter's amount (a change of level resizes them, a settings change being the only time anything is reallocated), so low simulates and draws a quarter, and low stops the trails flying. C6's per-shot beam, flash, light and CPU sparks stay as they were (tested since C6, and the net session draws remote shots through them); the A4 layer rides on them: remote players' projectiles run the same scripts on every screen (trails, debris), and their hitscan shots, which the net session draws as beams alone, spark where each beam ends (none twice for this player's own, none for a beam into the open), with scripts/net untouched. Muzzle bursts by family from the fire mode: pulse (the cannon), rail (the rail, a ring), scatter (SIGTERM and fork()), seeker (grep, a tight ring), launcher (fork bomb and cron, chunky embers) and heavy (rm -rf), made once with each model under its Muzzle. The viewmodels are drawn as at 80 degrees whatever the field of view (stretched across the view by the ratio of the two views' tangents) and shrunk to 0.25 toward the eye, which looks the same but keeps every model within 0.35 m of the eye, inside the player's 0.4 m radius, so no barrel pokes into a wall; the camera's near plane comes in to 0.01 m; the rig follows a shot jolt's lens offset by all but the shrink's share, so the shrunk models jolt on screen as full-sized ones would; beams start at the muzzle point (the marker grown back out from the eye to full size, on screen exactly the marker), and the C6 flash's light is placed in the world so its reach is not shrunk with it. Projectiles drawn from the muzzle, alone or in a match (the player's take_volley, which every volley of its own reaches): the slug, grep's darts and fork()'s pellets are seen leaving the barrel's tip and close on their true paths over 6 m; the path that counts still starts at the eyes; a fork bomb (lobbed) and a cron mine (placed) are not. SIGSTOP slows the arena's strips, beacons, screens and holograms through a second shader global, arena_clock, stepped by the dressing at the game's pace.
- Daemons fight back (producer, 2026-10-28 6:25 PM ET, after Fred's playtest asked 'will the monsters fight back'): chunk C27b gives solo play player health and real daemon AI with readable tells, ahead of C28; practice mode keeps the old never-attack dummies.
- C27b as built (ours, 2026-10-28): navigation is steering with raycasts, not a NavigationRegion3D: the arena is one open floor with a dozen boxes and a lair, and a daemon only chases what it can see (then the last place it saw it), so a straight seek, three feelers off ahead (0.6 rad apart, 1.6 m) and a slide along any wall it meets reach every place the hunt goes; a baked mesh would add a bake at every load and the navigation server to every test for paths no hunt takes (a daemon never goes round the lair's walls to find a player it cannot see). The fight code lives in scripts/fight/ (brain, director, bolt, solo life, tuning), apart from the art in scripts/daemons/, which the A2 and A2b scans keep cosmetic only. Sight is from a walker's eye 1.2 m up (the swarm's nest) to the player's middle against the level only; aggro 16 m, leash 14 m from its spot, sight lost for 2.5 s gives up, a hit rouses it at any range, a look every 0.2 s at home. Every attack is a tell, the strike, a recovery; the strike is entered only from a tell's end with the prey still attackable, in sight and in reach (one call site, checked in the test), else the tell ends in a recovery with nothing thrown; the strike's own checks run where it lands. rogue.d: 4.5 m/s, tells within 2.4 m (the old threat display, the rear-up, 0.6 s), lunges only as far as closes the gap to where its scythes come down (up to 1.4 m in 0.25 s) and slashes a player within 1.5 m of them with nothing of the level between its middle and the player, 20; 0.5 s recovery, 1.8 s before the next tell. The zombie: shambles at 1.4 m/s to 6 m, fires within 14 m: the claw raised with the ember pulled into it 0.7 s, then a bolt from the claw at where the player's middle was (its path checked first), 9 m/s, 0.3 m, 2.5 s, 12, stopped by the level, a daemon or a body it passes (an avatar's hit volume counting as that player); 2.6 s between. The swarm: 5.5 m/s on a 5 m circle at its spot's height, a tight bright ball 0.45 s before each dive, 12 m/s along the line to where the player was for at most 0.7 s or 1.5 m past it, 5 to a player its nest passes within 1 m of with nothing between, three dives a burst 0.35 s apart, 3 s after; any hit scatters it at 7 m/s for 1 s, ending the burst. The cap: two daemons per player hold a slot from the tell to the recovery's end. Difficulty: every hit times 0.6, 1 or 1.5, every wait between attacks divided by 0.75, 1 or 1.35; the tells never shorter. SIGSTOP freezes a daemon whole while its prey's SIGSTOP holds (alone the player's; in a match that player's, by the host's count): no step, no clock, its bolts hanging. Spawn protection: no tell starts on a protected player. Practice: the brains walk home and stand; the art's own displays come back; health is not shown. The solo life is the match's Combat authority for one player with resources/combat.tres's numbers (100, 3 s, 2 s, the flash), a daemon hit landing through Combat.damage_player with no killer credited; a death is the match's flow (set_alive(false), the derezz where the player stood; respawn_at the spawn farthest from the living daemons and set_alive(true)); the player's own weapons still never hurt it alone (the rm -rf cannon's self-damage stays a match's rule); a new round heals it. The HUD reuses the combat HUD's health line, flash, mint border and banner ("DEREZZED by rogue.d"), adds a colour-only vignette (0.9 at a hit, a 0.4 pulse under 35% health) and a 3.5 cm view jolt only with screen shake on. Round stats count deaths under the request whitelist's own "deaths", which comes first in its order, so it leads the stats line; SIGKILL's own roast and tip speak of 3 or more; the summary shows "derezzed N"; the solo top table shows the life's health and the daemons' hits and kills. The art: DaemonArt.drive() acts (move, tell, strike, recover, scatter), easing in and out over 0.25 s; a type's own displays only when no brain fights with it; rogue.d's planted feet carried back by the daemon's motion so they stay put in the world; the swarm's insects keep 55% of their own flight as it moves (they stream) and their trails all of it; the zombie's loop closes in to the daemon's place (its feet slide a little: see State). Hit volumes: rogue.d gains a 0.9 by 1 by 2.5 m BodyBox, head to tail, turning and moving with its art, beside the cylinder, off while it is down; its leg tips and the zombie's arms stay outside (thin and spread: a box round them would let shots between them land); the zombie's ghosts are its past and the swarm's cloud sits in its cylinder. Multiplayer: the host runs every daemon (its director against every player of the match) and judges every daemon hit in its combat; a joiner's copies follow _daemons (place, facing, state and its seconds, SIGSTOP's hold, life, health; 20 a second), throw _daemon_bolt's bolts to draw only, and refuse local hits; the host judges every player's shots at its daemons where it judges shots (hitscan, rail, pellets and darts, every blast, its own included as before); a daemon kill is the usual death with the cause "daemon:<n>", named on each screen from its own copy of the arena, feed "rogue.d: Daemon-2 / derezzed by a daemon"; a joiner's mirrored daemon death is nobody's kill on its screen; the rule (practice, difficulty) rides _rules, a joiner's daemons the old dummies until it comes; protocol 10, because no message before carried a daemon (each machine's dummies were its own). Settings: a gameplay section, difficulty and practice, under audio in the settings screen's middle column, the host's in a match. Captures: the c27b set (a rogue mid-lunge, its slash's vignette, a zombie's bolt in flight) from the player's own view, every other set shot in practice as before. From the Codex review: a hunt starts at the place its prey was spotted (never steering at an unknown point); every bolt stops when the director's mode changes (practice, a match starting or ending) and no daemon hit lands unless the brains decide on this machine; a bolt holds on the player it was thrown at; a bolt reaches a body only with nothing of the level between them; the host hunts and judges joiners at their newest heard place, not their drawn avatars; every bolt carries the host's id and the state word lists those in the air, so a joiner draws one it lost and stops one the host no longer has; a joiner's copy changes life only on the host's word; a joiner's hit on the host's daemon is told to it (_daemon_struck: its hit marker, a kill credited to it), every daemon death is told and only this player's own kills count as its own (`credited`): the sudo kill, the guide's kills, the round's processes down; a hit counts the health it took; four new messages in all, protocol 10. From the second review: a match begun with the player down alone sets it alive (the match counts it alive); bolts pass the players' avatars and meet each player where the host last heard it; the state word names whose kill each daemon's last death was (the death and its credit in one word; _daemon_struck only the hit marker) and carries each bolt's own SIGSTOP hold, a joiner's drawn bolt put where the host's is past 0.5 m; on the host of a match whose daemons fight, this player's own hits on them take root's scale; a match starting or ending and a daemon's round reset stop its bolts.
- ping (producer, 2026-10-28, the C28 brief): ping is weapon 9 (key 9), not a separate ability, the last free slot; the weapon strip's redesign (two rows or similar) comes with C29, not now. Fire sends one expanding sonar ring from the player, a cooldown and not ammo, its numbers in its weapon resource; every daemon inside the radius is pinged for a few seconds: a bright see-through outline and a small floating tag visible through walls, so daemons hunting you from behind cover are revealed. A ping that catches a daemon mid-tell (rogue.d rearing, a zombie charging a bolt, the swarm before a dive) interrupts that attack and resets it to a short stagger; it does no damage. In a match the host judges which daemons a ping hits through the existing combat and daemon path; a reveal of enemy players, if added, shows only on the pinger's screen; a protocol message and a bump only if truly needed. Practice: ping still reveals, with no tells to interrupt. A real viewmodel from the start, A4-style particles for the ring and the outline, and Fred's "would he say yawn" bar: a bold, readable, colourful pulse. Larry reviews each chunk's full diff himself while Codex is off.
- C28 as built (ours, 2026-10-28): the ring meets each daemon as its front reaches it (the distance from the pinger's middle to the daemon's middle, through walls), not all at the press, so the outline and the stagger arrive with the ring; a tell is caught only if it is still running when the front gets there. The stagger is the brain's scatter state, the word already on the wire: a walker never scattered before, and a walker in it now staggers (knocked back from the ping over the stagger's first 30%, reeling, glitching), the swarm's stagger is its own scatter; so a joiner draws it from the host's daemon stream and the protocol stays 10, with no new message (a new state would have been a new word an older joiner drops). After a stagger a walker waits its attack's own cooldown before a new tell, so a ping buys the stagger plus that wait; the swarm's burst is over and its burst gap follows. A ping is no hit: outside a tell it changes nothing and it rouses no daemon, in sight or not. The stagger's numbers are the daemons' own (resources/daemon_ai.tres, a Ping group: 1.0 s, 0.5 m); the ping's are its WeaponDef's (resources/ping_sonar.tres, a Ping group): a 7 s cooldown (the host's fire-rate check holds it in a match), an 18 m ring (past the daemons' 16 m sight, so it catches any daemon that could be hunting you) in 0.5 s, a 4 s reveal fading over its last 0.8 s, damage 0 and no magazine (WeaponDef refuses either). The outline is the daemon's own shell and core MultiMeshes drawn a second time with one shared no-depth-test material (rim, faint fill, climbing scan bands on the daemons' shared clock), under the art so it moves and poses with it and hides at a derezz; made once per daemon the first time a ping meets it, then kept: a stand-in capsule (code-rain vision's) would not read as the creature. The tag is a fixed-size Label3D with no depth test, the daemon's name and its distance from the camera, rewritten five times a second. Enemy players are never revealed: ping is sonar for daemons, and players have code-rain vision. In a match only the intent goes (as for the slug and cron): the host sends the ring from where it heard the pinger's body (not the claimed eye), judges its own daemons, and tells everyone with the seed message; every screen draws the ring, only the pinger's outlines. Fire is never a shot: no round shot, no accuracy, no meter. Colour lime (#b8ff2d, #e4ffb3), the one free hue (yellow-green, between the code rain's green and fork()'s yellow). The strip: the slot line a point smaller (10 px, as C25 to C27 did) so nine slots clear the guide panel at 960x540; ping's ammo line the cooldown, its third line the sonar coming back. The model is a sonar emitter whose dish's three rings are its charge pieces, lit by the cooldown. The ring: a floor ring (bright front, fading wash, echo lines), a low wall of light on its front that rises over the first 6 m so the pinger is never blinded (found in the capture: at 2 to 5 m it washed out the view), a new particle pool kind (ping, 2 emitters of 96, the cap 1536 of 1600) whose streaks fly out level at the ring's own speed, and a burst on each daemon met; the muzzle burst is the rail's ring.
- A5 as built (ours, 2026-10-29): solid bodies are a new armour look (shaders/daemon_armor.gdshader): near-black gunmetal #0d0f13, metallic 0.55, rough 0.32, each plate's tone 35% either way, a faint steel catch-light on true edges (0.24), a rim of the type's colour only where it turns away (0.15, power 5; at 0.7 the first captures read as glowing red and amber robots, so it came down), a scorched plate darker and matte; light only in the core look (seams, joints and weak points) at 0.6 of a core so it stays its colour in the glow; the glitch on armour at rest is calmer (glitch 0.015, tear 0.01, part 0.004 against 0.08, 0.05, 0.02) and as hard as ever on a hit or a respawn; glyphs and the derezz in each type's own colour; the obsidian (the shell look) is the zombie's peeling voxels now. Colours: rogue.d CRIMSON #ff1230 (deeper than the HUD's hostile red), the zombie hostile amber #ffb000, the runaway HOT_MAGENTA #ff1ba4 (redder than the fork bomb's #ff4dff; the glitch split stays #ff2bd6). New unit shapes in the shared kit: PLATE (a slab, bevelled top), LIMB (eight-sided, bevelled ends), BLADE (a curved scythe, a diamond in section), HEX (a hex puck, bevelled faces), RING (an eight-sided seam band). rogue.d: a flat hex thorax under 2 to 4 dorsal plates, a tail of 5 to 7 ribbed hex segments curling over its back with a crimson band between each, a stinger and its crimson bulb, a wedge head with one visor slit (1.1, flaring to 2.6 at the top of a rear), 0 to 2 horns, two scythes (upper arm, crimson elbow, blade, crimson wrist), six legs of 0.8 + 1.0 m (A2b's were 0.85 + 1.1: shorter and thicker reads heavier; the foot homes drawn in to 0.88 of A2b's so planted feet still reach the floor) with crimson knees; it crouches at 0.78 m and rears 0.38 m higher, 0.72 rad; the gait, tells and hit box as C27b. The zombie: armoured right (pelvis, belly, chest, pauldron, 1 to 3 back plates, 0 to 2 flank plates, 0 to 2 back spikes, a faceless head), open left (the ember in the cavity, a voxel arm of 6 to 9, 12 to 18 voxels peeling off that side, drifting up and out as they derezz), a cannon forearm with three charge bands lit in turn through the tell and a muzzle that swells; its limp at rest is its clock's (the clock checks hold), and driven its planted feet are carried in the world and its strides come as fast as it moves (its rate plus its speed over a 0.7 m stride, each landing half a stride ahead): C27b's foot slide is fixed, 0 mm measured while driven 5.6 m; hips 0.92 m, so its head stays in the head zone at every size. The runaway: 14 to 22 drones (hex pucks, two blade wings, a magenta light), a core drone (two armoured hex halves turning round a magenta band, its heart in the gap); killing the core scatters the swarm: the runaway's death is its core's, and the death's shatter flies each drone off whole as a group, 6 to 9 m/s out from the core and a little up plus 0.4 of its own flight, turning 2.5 rad/s, falling at a quarter of g, its other parts bursting as ever; the death is already the host's word in a match (the life word), so the scatter needs no message and no protocol of its own. No clones: a 31-bit build seed rolls every choice (size, plate and segment counts, horns, scythe length and hook, visor width, leg thickness and spread, crouch, gait timing; the zombie's bulk, hunch, pauldron, plates, spikes, voxels, stride rate, head; the swarm's drones, wings, orbit, core); battle damage 0 to 2 scorched plates and 0 to 3 flickering seams (the zombie's bad thigh always scorched and its bad knee always flickering; 0 to 2 drones' lights flickering). Elite: one spawn in twelve (DaemonArt.ELITE_RATE 1/12, a roll of the seed; measured 0.0849 over 12000 seeds); its size: rogue.d 1.18 (others 0.88 to 1.08), the zombie 1.08 (others 0.95 to 1.05, the head zone's bound), the runaway 1.12 (others 0.92 to 1.06); extra parts: rogue.d a second, smaller pair of scythes, three horns, a crimson seam down its back, 4 dorsal plates and 7 segments; the zombie bulk 1.15, three back spikes, a second pauldron plate, 3 back and 2 flank plates, 18 voxels shed; the runaway 30 drones, an armoured halo, a core 1.25 times; health: at least daemon_ai.tres elite_health 30 (the arena's daemons have 1: every hit kills; an elite lives through a cannon shot, 25, and a dummy with more health keeps its own). Size is the art's transform, scaled about the ground under it, so the feet stay down; rogue.d's body box and the tag scale with it; the hit cylinder is byte for byte as at 1afcdb7. Seeds: fixed by name (hash("sk:" + tag)) in demo, test and capture runs (--demo, or a --script run: every test and the capture tool), salted so the arena's four roll no elite and every older check keeps its one-hit kills (step 39 holds it); in play a new random seed each spawn, the art rebuilt in _begin_rematerialise while the body is nothing (its brain, box and tag told); a joiner's copy waits for the host's seed. In a match the host's _daemons carries each daemon's seed (a packed array of 32-bit numbers beside the rest): a changed message, so protocol 11 (one message changed, still 24 RPCs); a joiner's copy is rebuilt when its seed differs, before the rest of the word. Weak points marked in code (weak(), weak_points()): rogue.d's tail bands, knees, elbows, wrists, neck band, visor, stinger bulb and elite spine seam; the zombie's elbow, knees, charge bands, muzzle and ember; the runaway's core band and heart. Nothing aims at them yet (I2's exploit breaker will). Tests: step 39, tests/daemon_variety_test.gd (13 checks, 17 fail proofs, each exits 1); older tests: the protocol pins 10 to 11, the art tests on the new creatures (the zombie's ghost and bone checks became armour, shed voxels, a planted limp and the driven foot check; the swarm 12 to 40 drones; five shared materials), and rm -rf's blast test puts the two zombies nearest and rogue.d farthest (rogue.d's body box wanders with its idle yaw and caught the slug short of the wall once A5's seeds changed its phases). Captures: the a5 set, the line-up and the elites from each creature's own art (no dummy round it) standing or in their tells, facing the camera, so builds compare like for like; a5-scatter is one more than the brief's five.
- I1 as built (ours, 2026-10-29): ICE is its own class beside the daemons (scripts/ice/: a director beside the arena, IcePiece bodies, IceArt art on A5's rig), off in practice and with the new Settings, Gameplay "ICE" switch (on by default; in a match the host's, riding the rules message). Palette, measured in OKLab: white ICE glacier #18b2ff (the brief's #3d8bff was 0.035 from grep's cobalt #4d7dff, so it moved toward azure), hunter cobalt #1e30ff, the hunter's HUD line #9aa6ff, every piece's glass #08121f; glacier's nearest are grep's cobalt and the crosshair's cyan at 0.136, then teal 0.143 and the rail's ice 0.203; cobalt's nearest is grep's cobalt at 0.166, then violet 0.208; the two 0.29 apart (step 40 holds each at least 0.12 from teal, mint, the rail's two, lime, crimson, amber, hot magenta, grep's cobalt and violet). Look: faceted near-black glass (metal 0.2, rough 0.12, specular 0.8, tone 30% either way), a facet's inner light 0.1 (a few facets hold most of it, power 3), seams' line 0.07 at 1 px fading to 0.2 of itself from 4 to 16 m (so nothing reads as wire), rim 0.12 (power 3), cracks 1.1 (cells 3 a unit, width 0.035, a third toward white at their core), a hit's flare 2.0 on edges and rim only (a wall never flashes whole: its many seams lit at once bloomed into a white sheet in the first captures), light (seams, joints, cores) the core shader at 3.2 with a 0.15 breath at 0.35 Hz and no glitch, its flash 2.5; a node's dome: rim 2.4, lattice 1.1 at 26 cells, flash 6. New shapes: FACET (a hex pane with a cut crown), PRISM (a double-terminated crystal), SHIELD (FACET's mesh in its own set, so only a hunter's shields and spines take the adaptation's colour); new looks ICE and ICE_LIGHT. Layout (arena space): the gate fills the room's doorway (0, -8; 2.7 by 3.5 m: rogue.d's lair sealed until it breaks), the wall the west corridor's north end (-17.6, 8; 4.8 by 3 m); the jam node at (-4.5, -4.5) before the gate, the HUD node at (-17.5, -2) south of the corridor (13 m and more from every spawn); the hunter's post (10, -4), facing the middle. A wall's body is a skin on the targets' layer (shots, pellets, darts, slugs land on it; the rail stops at it after its hit) round a core on the level's (players, daemons, sight and bolts stop at it). Numbers (resources/ice.tres): walls 600 health, nodes 150, the hunter 300; no one hit takes more than 100 off any piece (hit_cap: the rail's 1000 counts 100); jam node: trigger 9 m, tell 1.2 s, field 7 m, jam 2.5 s, wait 7 s; HUD node: trigger 10 m, tell 1.4 s, field 8 m, corruption 4 s, wait 9 s; a field's dome lit 0.6 s after it fires; a tell is never under 1 s (TELL_FIRST_S, refused by the tuning); nodes' waits divided by the daemons' aggression (easy 0.75, hard 1.35); hunter from round 2, one at a time, speed 3.6 m/s, keeps 9 m, hovers at 1.35 m, sees 26 m with no leash, keeps a lost intruder's last place 6 s, starts its lance within 16 m, reaches 22 m, tells 1.3 s with the trace held still for the last 0.35 s, hits within 0.55 m of the line for 24 (the difficulty's damage scale on it, through the daemons' combat path and attacker cap), strike 0.25 s, recovery 0.7 s, wait 3.2 s (over aggression), back 15 s after a kill, size 1.3 to 1.42 (its middle kept at 1.35 m), hit volume a 1.05 m cylinder 1.6 m tall; adaptation: the most dealt weapon this round (hits on daemons and on ICE alike, each counted up to the cap) resisted up to 50% at 500 damage (adapt_max, never above 0.9 by the tuning's own check: never immunity), shown "HUNTER ADAPTED: <weapon> -NN%" and in its shields shifting toward the weapon's colour (85% of the way at the cap); a new round forgets it. Fair: every field and lance has a readable tell and lands only on a player in its reach and in sight at the tell's end, spawn protection holds, a ping mid-tell staggers the hunter as it does a daemon. Hooks for I2: every hit carries its source (the weapon's id; TargetDummy.hit and IcePiece.hit take it), the brute breaker's harder hits on a wall are a hook in the director (BRUTE_SCALE 3, source "breaker:brute"), weak points marked with weak() (a wall's lock, rings and joints, a node's heart and rings, the hunter's heart). Seeds: fixed by name ("ice:" + piece) in demo, test and capture runs, a new one each round in play, the host's in a match. In a match: protocol 12, two new messages (the host's ICE word at the sync rate: round, each piece's seed, health, state and time, the hunter's place, yaw, aim and reach, the adaptation; and a field's effect sent to the one joiner it reached), the rules carry ICE on or off; joiners' shots and blasts at ICE are judged by the host (its _is_daemon takes ICE), a joiner's jammed weapon is refused (combat's DROP_JAM) until the jam clears, a death by the lance is "hunter ICE" in the feed. The credit: "ICE design: @TimProvencio", small, top right under the guide HUD's lines, while ICE is on (the Audit Trail visor's tag, PRD 5.8, is not built yet; this follows its pattern). The HUD corruption covers the status line, the round line, the weapon strip, the sudo badge and the kill feed, never the health line. Captures: i1-white-ice (the corridor's wall at 62% damage, three panes out, the cannon's beam striking), i1-hunter (round 2, mid-tell, adapted to the cannon at 34%), and beyond the brief's two: i1-field (the jam node's dome), i1-corrupted (the HUD scrambled, health untouched) and i1-lineup (three hunters and two of each node from other seeds).
- I2 as built (ours, 2026-10-29): ICEbreakers are one-use programs carried beside the weapons, never in a weapon slot or on the 1 to 9 strip (scripts/ice/: breakers.gd the rules, breaker_director.gd beside ICE's director, decoy.gd, breaker_pickup.gd, breaker_art.gd on A5's rig, exploit_marks.gd, breaker_pick.gd; numbers in resources/breakers.tres). Carried: 1 of each kind when a round starts, at most 2 of a kind, 0.35 s between two runs. Keys: U runs the loaded breaker, Y loads the next kind, skipping a kind with none left while any is held; both rebindable ("run breaker", "next breaker"). The brief's H was not free: the multiplayer panel's H (with J, K and L) is kept from every action since C13 (step 11 pins it), so the run is on U, the nearest free key beside Y; Y was free (the guide's yes counts only while its question is up, and the question takes it first); root and sudo's test moved its rebind target from U to P. brute: a charge from the eye along the aim, reaching what is within 4.5 m (a wall's nearest point) inside a 40 degree cone with nothing of the level between, at full strength within 2.25 m and down to 50% at 4.5 m; 120 on a node, the hunter or a daemon, past ICE's 100 cap (its own numbers cap it), and a wall takes 5 times it, 600: a whole wall breaks up close, one from 3.4 m is left at about a quarter; never resisted, never noted for the hunter's adaptation (no breaker is), an attack (it ends a cloak), and it hits daemons too (a joiner's brute kill is theirs). I1's hook reworked: its BRUTE_SCALE 3 became the breaker tuning's brute_wall_scale 5 (3 times the 100 cap was 300, half a wall), the source "breaker:brute" kept. exploit: 6 s in which a hit on a weak seam (A5's weak() points, a daemon's and ICE's) within 0.4 m at the target's size lands 2.5 times harder, after ICE's hit cap (the rail's 1000 on a wall's seam: 100 then 250), direct hits only (the cannon, SIGTERM, the rail, fork(), grep; never a fork bomb, rm -rf's slug, a cron mine's blast or ping); on this screen only, a pulsing ultraviolet diamond over every seam of every daemon alive and piece of ICE standing (one MultiMesh, one draw call; 59 in the capture). cloak: 5 s in which no node starts its tell on the intruder or reaches it with a field, and no daemon or hunter spots it, sees it or ends a tell on it (a hunt falls back to where it was last seen, home after its lose time; a tell ends with nothing thrown); a strike already thrown (a lunge, a bolt, a dive, a lance) still lands; any shot or throw the host accepts (alone, the player's own), or a brute, ends it at once; a death ends it; on screen "CLOAKED 4.9s  firing ends it" under the crosshair and the screen's edges washed ultraviolet (0.5), both blinking at 4 Hz in its last second. decoy: thrown along the aim at 9 m/s plus 3 m/s of lift under 16 m/s2, settling where it lands (a wall drops it to the floor under the strike; 2 s of flight at most), so aimed at the floor it drops at the feet; it calls for 7 s once landed: the hunter within 26 m goes after it, whatever it hunts, mid-tell too (its trace swings to the decoy unless it has locked, and its lance is judged on the decoy, never on the intruder it left); every daemon within 26 m whose leash allows goes after it at home, hunting or going home, never mid-attack; a node with it inside its trigger range tells on it and its field goes into it, reaching no one; the call needs no sight; what strikes it only flashes it; bolts meet it as they meet players; no shot meets it (it has no body); 4 decoys at once arena-wide, a new one taking the oldest's slot; its end a burst of its own parts over an ultraviolet derezz. The decoy pulls daemons too (the brief named ICE), so it is an escape with the ICE switch off as well. Pickups: six spots in arena space, (16, 0), (-11, -4), (5, 4.5), (-4, 16), (17, -10) and (-5, -13) (inside rogue.d's room, behind the gate: a reason to break in); the first 15 s into a round, then one every 20 s while fewer than 2 are out, at a seeded free spot with a seeded kind (fixed by the round in demo, test and capture runs); an intruder within 1.4 m takes it (+1) unless that kind is at its cap, when it stays; a new round clears them. Palette, measured in OKLab: the breakers' ultraviolet #7400cc, found by a search of the hue wheel for the farthest point from every bright colour of the palette (its nearest: the hunter's cobalt 0.161, SIGTERM's violet 0.165), and its HUD text #c27fff (lightness 0.72, 0.10 from violet and the hunter's line); step 41 holds the ultraviolet at least 0.12 from every bright palette colour. Look: a pickup is a split key crystal (two armoured prism halves round a burning ultraviolet core, a seam of light round each half), four facet plates orbiting it, five glyphs, a hex plinth with a ring of light, a beam of light 2.4 to 2.9 m tall standing from it (the trail look) so it reads across the arena, its kind's emblem over it (brute a ram of two blades, exploit a lens of a ring round a gem, cloak a hood of two panes round a dim core, decoy twin shards bound by a ring) and its kind's name on a tag; no shadow (small and hovering; 900 triangles, 8 draw calls). The decoy is a faceless effigy of the intruder's signal: a crystal spine on a hex plinth, torso plates front and back, shoulder panes, a head of light, three seam rings climbing the spine in turn and two call rings rolling out over the floor to 3.2 m every 0.9 s, its light stuttering in its last quarter; folded small and tumbling in flight; 898 triangles, 11 draw calls. Every pickup and decoy is built from a seed (the host's in a match). The HUD: bottom left, right over the sudo badge (the corner of the player's own abilities): "U BRUTE 1" (dimmed with none left) over "Y [b1] e1 c1 d1", a notice over them for 1.6 s ("cloak run", "no brute left", "+1 decoy"); not beside the strip because at 960x540 the column over the strip is the kill feed's (it cannot rise without going under the multiplayer panel, as the weapon and combat tests caught) and the guide panel's centred column takes the room to its left, so the strip keeps its whole column for C29's second bank; I1's HUD corruption scrambles the readout too. The guide's pick: built only from the round's numbers (breaker_pick.gd: round_number, ice_walls, ice_wall_health_pct, ice_nodes, ice_node_health_pct, hunter_out, hunter_health_pct, hunter_adapted_weapon (an arsenal id or "none"), hunter_adapted_pct, breakers_brute, breakers_exploit, breakers_cloak, breakers_decoy), each in guide_request.gd's whitelist; a request carrying them adds the fixed BREAKER_LINE to its instructions. On T, while breakers are carried, every question carries them, so the AI also names a breaker, and Enter with nothing typed asks for the pick alone; between rounds the round's one call (coach or roast) carries them and the summary panel's pick line is the AI's when its line names a breaker ("next round, load decoy (your AI's pick)"), else the scripted guide's; the AI off, refused, failed or silent: the scripted guide's pick follows the notice ("Scripted guide: Load brute: 2 ICE walls up. Up close, one charge breaks a wall.").
