Scripted rules: the hunter out and adapted 25% or more with a decoy held, decoy; the hunter out with an exploit, exploit; walls standing with a brute, brute; nodes standing with a cloak, cloak; else the first held of exploit, cloak, decoy and brute; none held, "No breakers left: find a pickup, the ultraviolet beams."; demo mode answers with the scripted pick. Practice turns breakers off (no readout, no pickups, a press dropped, never kept for later); the ICE switch does not (they work on the daemons). In a match: protocol 13, two new messages: a joiner's ask (any_peer, untyped and type-checked: its life, the kind, its aim; the host runs it from its own view of the joiner's eye and refuses a wrong life, a dead player, an unknown kind, none held or too soon) and the host's breaker word to each joiner at the sync rate (unreliable, ordered: its counts, its exploit and cloak seconds left, the four decoys' places, calls and landings, the six spots' kinds, the last brute charge's count, place and way, that joiner's own last run and pickup, and every decoy's and pickup's seed, so its screen builds the host's); 28 RPCs and 31 sends, 8 any_peer; the host's shot and throw checks end the shooter's cloak. Tests: step 41, tests/breaker_test.gd (38 checks; 26 deliberate breaks each fail it); older tests' pins: protocol 13, 28 RPCs and 31 sends, 8 any_peer (combat), ping's 28, root and sudo's rebind target, the graphics test's I2_TRES. Captures: i2-breakers (exploit's marks lit on the corridor's wall and a zombie while a cloak runs, both effect lines, the readout), i2-decoy (the hunter's lance going into a decoy thrown off to the side, the player in its sight untouched), and beyond the brief's two: i2-brute (the charge breaking the wall up close) and i2-lineup (the four pickups and a decoy from other seeds).
- I3 as built (ours, 2026-10-29): Black ICE, the machine's last line of defence, one more piece of ICE beside the walls, the nodes and the hunter, run by ICE's director (scripts/ice/: black_ice.gd the piece, black_ice_brain.gd, black_ice_art.gd on A5's rig, black_ice_tuning.gd, blackout.gd; scripts/hud/black_ice_hud.gd; numbers in resources/black_ice.tres). Rarity: never in round 1; from round 2 its chance is rolled once a round, 20% in round 2, 10 points more each round after, 40% at the most (round 4 on); when it comes it arrives at a seeded moment 20 to 40 s into the round's play (the round HUD's own clock, playing(): it stops while the player is away), at whichever of five spots is farthest from the nearest intruder: (16, -4), (-13, -7), (11, -9), (3, -3) and (-6, 17) in arena space, open floor at least 5.8 m from every spawn; at most one a round (a broken one does not come back, a summoned one clears the round's plan, the round's end withdraws one still out); fixed by the round in demo, test and capture runs (the fixed plan brings it in rounds 3, 4, 5, 9 and 11, 28.6 to 36.8 s in), a new roll each round in play, the host's in a match. Its arrival: a 6 s tell (never under 3), on every screen: the warning centred high ("B L A C K   I C E" flickering in, "the machine's last defence: weapons only flash it, its touch flatlines", "it forms in 4.2 s   18 m away"); the arena's lights going down over its first 2 s to 10% of themselves: a new shader global, arena_light (project.godot), multiplied into the arena's own shaders (the strips, signs and beacons, the panels' edges, the screens and racks, the holograms, the floor, the walls' rain and the skyline), and the sun, the room's light, the signs' lamps, the lair's danger lights (ArenaArt.light_level) and the environment's ambient, sky and fog light scaled with it; Black ICE's own light, the daemons', ICE's, the weapons' and the HUD untouched; the arena's hum (new: a quiet loop of a low server-room drone on the Music bus, which had nothing on it, 20 dB under its slider) dropping out with the lights; the guide's link shown cut ("your AI: LINK CUT" flickering in Black ICE's text colour, the latency and GPU lines dark, "NO CARRIER   Black ICE on the line"), cosmetic only: no AI call is made or cancelled for it, T still asks and its answer still shows; a deep 6 s fall of a sound; its body forming out of nothing (a dashed ring of cold light at its touch's reach first, the core's plates closing in, the spikes and legs growing out, the seals lit last); nothing touches before the tell ends. Everything comes back over 1.5 s once it is broken or withdrawn. Out: it hunts by sight from its core (2.6 m up, over the low cover) to 45 m, no leash, straight at its prey at 4 m/s (a running player does 7; the tuning refuses 5.6 or more), keeps a lost intruder's last place 6 s, and with no one in sight drifts toward the nearest intruder it is not hidden from at 2.4 m/s; it never tells or strikes; a low drone comes from it (unit size 6 m). Touch: an intruder's middle within 1.65 m of its axis (its 1.25 m body and a player's 0.4) and under its crown, out of spawn protection, is flatlined, cloaked or not (the cloak hides you from its sight, never from its body). Alone: SoloLife.flatline() takes all the health through the combat path, holds the player down (no respawn, no countdown: "FLATLINED by Black ICE   the run is over"), and the round ends at once as a loss: the break's summary headed "FLATLINED by Black ICE   RUN OVER   round 1 in 12 s" in hostile red over the screen darkened to 60%, the death banner giving it the room; the run is over: the next round is round 1 and brings the player back. In a match: the host's combat takes it all at once (the seventh host-only damage site), a death with its own cause ("ice:black": the feed "Black ICE: <name>" / "flatlined: out until the round ends"), held down by the host until its ICE starts the next round, spectating as top meanwhile ("[ FLATLINED by Black ICE ]   out until the round ends"). Weapons: every hit and blast on it flashes it (a cold flicker of its edges, a spray of shards) and takes nothing: no hit counted, nothing noted for the hunter's adaptation, exploit's bonus on a seal still nothing. Only the combo: a brute charge reaching it while that intruder's own exploit runs breaks one of its 2 seals, throwing it back 3 m along the charge over 1.2 s (in which it hunts nothing; "SEAL BROKEN   1 left"); the last broken kills it ("BLACK ICE DOWN", a burst of its parts over a derezz in its colour); a broken seal mends after 12 s unless the last goes first. So a kill takes two brutes inside one exploit's 6 s or two combos inside the mend, and getting within the brute's 4.5 m: more than a round's starting kit (one of each), so a pickup or a teammate. A brute without exploit only flashes it. A decoy pulls it (no leash: any decoy calling within the breakers' 26 m) and holds it while it calls; the cloak hides you from it as from the hunter. Look: a black sun of ICE, 5.1 m to its top and 5.5 m across its spikes: a core of 10 to 12 black crystal plates (FACET) on a sphere round a dim cold heart (its light only in the gaps between the plates and along 3 to 5 thin veins), 18 to 24 long black spikes (PRISM) radiating every way but straight down and slowly breathing, 6 to 8 spike legs reaching down to the floor in a ring at its touch's reach (so its footprint is its touch), two seals as rings of cold light crossing round the core and turning, and the dashed floor ring; nothing of a face; per spawn, seeded: the spikes (count, ways, lengths, breathing), the legs (count, places), the core's plates and turn, the seals' tilt and turn, the veins. Colour #2a6e81, a cold dead slate (the farthest point from every bright palette colour in a search of the cold hues 175 to 235 degrees under chroma 0.075: 0.200 from grep's cobalt, 0.243 from glacier; step 42 holds it at least 0.15 from every bright colour), its HUD text #77bdd3, its glass #020304; ICE's crystal shader with Black ICE's own material (facet light 0.01, seam line 0.08, rim 0.3 at power 4.5, rough 0.45, specular 0.25, no metal, a hit's flash 0.5: light falls into it, its silhouette a thin cold rim) and ICE's light with its own energy (1.6, a 0.25 breath at 0.18 Hz). The HUD: the warning; out, top centre at 150 px "BLACK ICE   seals 2/2   14 m" (a broken seal's "mends in 9 s") over the scripted guide's word; a tag over it ("[ BLACK ICE 14 m ]", at the screen's left or right edge with an arrow when it is off screen); the screen's edges washed cold within 12 m of its touch, pulsing faster as it nears; "SEAL BROKEN" and "BLACK ICE DOWN" called out; a flatline flashes the screen in its colour. The AI: four new whitelisted numbers (black_ice_out, black_ice_seals, black_ice_distance_m, black_ice_flatlined) in the breaker pick's numbers, and a fixed BLACK_ICE_LINE added to a request with it out (recommend the decoy, or the exploit and brute combo when the brutes cover its seals); the scripted guide while it is out: the combo when this player holds an exploit and enough brutes for its seals, else the decoy, else the cloak, else run. Practice and the ICE switch turn it off. In a match: protocol 14, no new message: ICE's own word carries Black ICE's (its seed and 12 numbers: phase, seconds into it, seals, place in arena space, yaw, brain state and time, SIGSTOP's hold, the hits that flashed it, the mend), so it never runs out of step with the rest of ICE (a separate message, tried first, was a second unreliable packet each tick and made step 40's timing checks flaky); a joiner builds it from the host's seed and plays its word; a joiner's combo asks are judged by the host from where it last heard the joiner's eyes; 28 RPCs and 31 sends still. Sounds: Black ICE's are made once, at the start of the first round it can come in (about 150 ms of synthesis), never at its arrival. Cost (I3, measured on a real GPU at 1920x1080): one Black ICE 2,192 triangles in 7 draw calls, three drawn and hidden in turn no measurable GPU time (1.80 to 2.01 ms against 1.66 to 1.86 ms) for 15 draw calls and 6.8k primitives, posing 183 microseconds a frame at the quickest of five rounds (median 201). The first look, a robed wraith of blades, read as a blue armoured gown in the stills (would Fred say yawn: yes) and was dropped for the black sun before the first commit. Tests: step 42, tests/black_ice_test.gd; older tests' pins: protocol 14 and the refusal line "host 14", the ICE word's longer signature (net), seven host-only damage sites (combat), black_ice.tres beside the other tunings (graphics).
- C29 as built (ours, 2026-10-29): the weapon strip's second bank and chmod 000, the freeze ray, weapon 10 (scripts/weapons/: frost.gd a target's freeze meter, coolant.gd the tank, freeze_beam.gd the beam as drawn, freeze_tuning.gd; scripts/fx/frost_look.gd; scripts/hud/freeze_hud.gd; scripts/viewmodels/chmod_000.gd; shaders/frost_crust.gdshader and freeze_beam.gdshader; every number in resources/chmod_000.tres, the WeaponDef with its FreezeTuning inside it). Keys: Shift was free (no action, no fixed key, nothing in the settings' defaults: SIGKILL has no sprint, it runs by default), so the bank key is Shift held with 1 to 9: a new rebindable action, weapon_bank, shown "weapon bank 2" in the settings' key list; Shift+1 draws weapon 10; Q and the wheel reach every slot of both banks (the wheel runs 1 to 10 and wraps); ArsenalTuning holds 18, two banks of 9 (BANK_SLOTS); slot keys 1 to 9 stay fixed as since C16. Layout: the strip keeps its whole column bottom right; bank 2's row is one label over bank 1's row, the bank key's name then its keys ("Shift+ [1 chmod]", the drawn one bracketed and named), bank 1's row unchanged ("1 2 3 4(2) 5 6 7 8 9"), both at the slot line's 10 px; the active bank (the drawn weapon's, or bank 2 while the bank key is held, since 1 to 9 then draw its weapons) lit, the other at half; at 960x540 the strip's top is 88 px over its bottom gap, so the kill feed rose 8 px (FEED_BOTTOM_GAP 104 to 112): it clears the new row by 2 px and a full feed's top stays 3 px under the spectate table. The weapon: fire_mode FREEZE, new after PING (BEAM stays reserved for the pipe's chain beam): fire held, not clicked; a beam tick every 0.1 s (fire_cooldown) along the aim to the first thing within 22 m (fire_range); no damage (damage 0, the tuning refuses more) and no magazine: a coolant tank of 4 s of beam, resting 0.6 s after the last tick, refilling empty to full in 3 s, venting when run dry (no beam until back to 40%); root's free ammo spends none; the beam starts only from a fire press made while engaged (the click that grabs the mouse never beams). Never a shot: no accuracy, no shot counted. The meter (Frost): a tick gives the target its 0.1 s of beam; a daemon fills in 1 s (an elite in 1.5, the hunter and the nodes in 1.6, a player in 1.5); it holds 0.35 s once the beam leaves, then melts (full to empty in 1.2 s); full, the target is frozen 3 s (ICE 2.2, a player 1) and empties; after the thaw a guard of 1.5 s (a player 2.5) in which the beam builds nothing, so no chain of freezes. Frozen: a daemon does nothing at all (no step, no turn, no attack; DaemonBrain._held_by_chmod holds its clock and every timer); a tell or a strike it was in is called off (its slot let go, "frozen" noted, the cooldown its attack's own wait, as a ping's stagger); a bolt already thrown flies on; its art holds its pose (DaemonArt.frozen). The hit rule (picked): the next hit on a frozen daemon thaws it and lands frozen_hit_scale 2x; a hit at least shatter_damage 40 strong (before any scale: the rail, rm -rf, a cron run) shatters it outright whatever its health; and since a plain daemon has 1 health, any kill of a frozen daemon is a shatter: its derezz in the frost opal, a storm of frost chips. ICE: the hunter freezes like a daemon (its lance's tell called off), a node's field is paused (a tell, or a field's dome just fired, called off into its cooldown, its clock held while frozen, then its cooldown waited out); frozen ICE's next hit lands 2x after the hit cap (exploit's place: the rail's 1000 is 100 then 200); ICE never shatters; walls have no meter ("chmod: read-only file system"). Black ICE is only slowed while the beam holds: 40% of its pace in 0.6 s of beam, at most 40% (the tuning refuses more), wearing off over 0.8 s after the 0.35 s hold; never frozen ("chmod: Operation not permitted"), never hurt, never flashed, its touch and its seals untouched (only the exploit and brute combo breaks them); a faint frost over it, at 60% of the creep at the full slow. Players (a match): slowed by up to 45% as the meter fills (the pace scales the move, a slide never starts slowed), then frozen 1 s: no move, jump, slide, fire, throw, draw, reload, breaker, SIGSTOP or sudo, looking stays free; the host refuses its shots, throws and breaker asks ("frozen"); a 2.5 s guard after; spawn protection holds (no meter on a protected player); no damage bonus on a frozen player (fair). Colour: the frost opal #a87884 (its HUD text and the skin's beam colour #e196a2, its glints and the beam's thin core #f5d0d6): every blue and cyan was taken (the player's teal, the rail's ice, grep's cobalt, glacier, the hunter's cobalt, Black ICE's slate), so a search of the whole colour cube (bright enough to glow, no Matrix greens, no hostile reds) for the farthest point (OKLab) from every bright palette colour found a dusty rose opal: nearest cron's rose 0.174, rm -rf's ember 0.175, Black ICE's slate 0.176; glacier 0.225, the hunter's cobalt 0.33, the breakers' ultraviolet 0.28; step 43 holds it 0.15 from every bright colour and 0.17 from ICE's, Black ICE's and the breakers' colours and texts; its text lightness 0.75. Look: the model is a hexagonal emitter in hex-cell metal, a glass coolant tank on top whose four cells (its charge pieces) light with the coolant left, three notched lock tumblers round the barrel (r, w and x) turning at rest and spun hard while the beam runs, three cold prongs round a breathing crystal lens, "000" in three lit rings on its flank, cold fins (1,440 triangles, under the 3,000 budget; all ten models 7,084); the beam (one per shooter, made once): a thin hot core and a frost-opal sheath (shaders/freeze_beam.gdshader, light running in bands toward the target), a helix of 18 frost shards down it, a bloom where it lands and its own 24-particle spray, a cold synthesised hiss; the frost (FrostLook, made the first time the beam meets a target): the target's own shell, core and armour MultiMeshes drawn again as a shell of ice (shaders/frost_crust.gdshader: see-through in the middle so the dark creature and its seams show inside, thick at the silhouette, a 3D Voronoi of crystal cells whose cracks burn in the text colour, a few cells winking, glossy), creeping up from the feet with the meter in a ragged front, sealed and lit when frozen, and a tag drawn over everything ("chmod 000  64%", then "PERMISSION DENIED" over "---------- 000" and the seconds); a FROST particle pool (2 x 32: the arena's cap now 1600 of the 1600 budget) bursts chips off the body at a freeze, fewer at a thaw, all at a shatter; a frost muzzle family; under the crosshair (grep's place) what the beam does ("chmod 000  rogue.d [#####.....]", "PERMISSION DENIED  rogue.d  2.9s", Black ICE's and a wall's refusals, "chmod: thawing, retry in 0.9s", "coolant venting"); a frozen player's own screen frosts at its edges and says "chmod 000: Permission denied" over "you are read-only  0.6s". Match: protocol 15, no new message (28 RPCs; 32 sends, one more: a player's own freeze meter sent back to it). A tick is the usual fire intent, believed by the usual checks plus two: the host keeps every player's coolant (a little more lenient: the fire-rate tolerance as slack) and freeze meter; it casts the tick's ray itself (_beam_freeze) and judges the players it meets and, while the daemons fight, a joiner's beam on its daemons and ICE (Black ICE only slowed); the host's own beam lands on its arena as its shots do. The meters ride words already sent: a daemon's and a piece of ICE's in their state byte (bits 3 to 5 the meter in eighths, bit 6 frozen, beside the state's three bits and SIGSTOP's), Black ICE's slow as its word's thirteenth number, and every player's in the movement word (_remote_state gained a frost byte; the host also sends a joiner its own while it has any). Every screen draws a remote tick from the shot message (_shot, no hits) as the avatar's beam. I1's known issues fixed on the way: ping outlines the hunter, the nodes and Black ICE through the level as it does a daemon (the walls stand in plain sight); grep may lock the hunter and the nodes (alone; never a wall or Black ICE; in a match its marks are players). Tests: step 43, tests/freeze_ray_test.gd (40 checks); older tests' pins extended: protocol 15 and "host 15", 32 sends, ten weapons, the strip's 14 labels, the wheel through slot 10, _remote_state's signature, the arsenal's pin with chmod's lines out and chmod_000.tres beside the other tunings (graphics), ten models and families (viewmodel, graphics). Captures: c29-freeze (rogue.d frozen in its tell under the beam), c29-strip (the strip's two rows cut from that frame, 3x), and beyond the brief's two: c29-chill (a zombie half frozen, the meter under the crosshair), c29-shatter (the cannon on a frozen daemon), c29-hunter (the hunter frozen) and c29-black (Black ICE only slowed). The first stills failed the art bar (a beam drawn as a vertical line from a world-axis scaling bug, a near-white plaster crust and a snow of chips) and were redone before the stills were kept.
- C30 as built (ours, 2026-10-29): pipe |, the chain beam, weapon 11 (bank 2's second: Shift+2; Q and the wheel reach it; the strip "Shift+ 1 [2 pipe]"), fire_mode BEAM (the mode C29 kept for it), PRD 5.2's "chain beam that arcs between enemies" (scripts/weapons/: pipe_chain.gd the rules, pipe_buffer.gd the ammo, pipe_arc.gd the chain as drawn, pipe_tuning.gd; scripts/hud/pipe_hud.gd; scripts/viewmodels/pipe_chain.gd; shaders/pipe_arc.gdshader; every number in resources/pipe_chain.tres, the WeaponDef with its PipeTuning inside it). Picked: one chain a click (not held: chmod already is, and a chain is one event to judge, draw and read), one every 0.45 s; the aim's ray reaches 30 m and its first target is link 1 (26 damage); from each link the stream jumps to the nearest target not yet in the chain within 12 m of the link's middle (middle to middle) with nothing of the level between them (level layer only: walls, cover and ICE walls' cores), up to 5 links (6 as root, C22's pattern: one more of the weapon's own thing); each link 0.7 of the one before (26, 18.2, 12.74, 8.92, 6.24: a plain daemon dies to any link, two players in reach lose 26 and 18.2); the damage lands at once, the look races 0.04 s a link. The jump is 12 m so a chain links practice's two nearest dummies (the zombie and the runaway, 11.8 m apart) and every crowd that closes in. Frozen link (chmod, C29): a daemon or a piece of ICE that chmod 000 holds frozen conducts: the stream passes through it losing nothing (frozen_keep 1.0, refused under link_keep) and jumps 1.5x as far from it (18 m), while its own hit is C29's rule (thawed, 2x; a plain daemon shattered); a frozen player conducts nothing (C29's fairness: nothing extra for a frozen player). Each link after the first lands on its target's weak seam nearest the stream's way in (A5's weak points: the data finds an open port), so I2's exploit counts on every link (2.5x on a seam, after ICE's cap); a player's link lands on its middle. ICE: the hunter and the nodes are links (the hunter's adaptation notes the pipe like any weapon); a wall hit first takes its hit and passes nothing on, and no chain ever jumps to one; Black ICE hit first only flashes (its own hit rule: nothing taken), "pipe: broken pipe", and nothing goes on; no chain ever jumps to it (never in the pool), so its seals and touch stay I3's. The chain never jumps through a body to the target behind it either: only the first target is the aim's; the rest are picked by reach and the level's sight alone. A spawn-protected or dead player is never a link; hit first, nothing lands and nothing goes on. Ammo (picked): the pipe's buffer, not a magazine (a pipe's own fiction, and chmod's held coolant has the bank's other meter): 64K, each chain writes 8K (eight chains), drains 24K a second after a 0.5 s rest (just over the 0.45 s interval, so fire at the full rate never drains), and a full buffer blocks writes until it has drained to 50% ("pipe  BLOCKED", "blocked [##########]", "pipe: write blocked, buffer full"); root writes nothing; the host keeps every player's on its own clock with the fire-rate tolerance's slack ("buffer" drop). Readout (scripts/hud/pipe_hud.gd): under the crosshair at grep's place, one row (the breakers' effect lines stay under it at +52 px): the chain at a prompt, "$ rogue.d | zombie | runaway", and its count beside it ("3 links", "1 link: no one else in reach", "1 link: a wall broke the chain"), a frozen link as "zombie(000)", a wall hit first "$ white ICE" with "pipe: the wall reads nothing on", Black ICE "pipe: broken pipe" with "Black ICE takes nothing", for 1.8 s fading over its last 0.5 s; a row too wide is drawn at 13 px, then its names cut to 12 and 7 characters ("~"), so a root's six 24-character player names fit 960x540. The strip: "pipe  buffer 24K/64K" over "buffer [####......]" in the pipe's text colour. Look: the model is a length of pipe made a weapon (1,812 triangles, 13 of its pieces lit or turning on their own; all eleven models 8,896): bolted flanges, brass conduits, a buffer gauge of eight lit "|" bars on the eye's flank (lit as the buffer fills), two arrows down the pipe, three segmented couplings and a valve wheel that turn slowly and spin hard after each chain, a tee whose side outlets end in glowing rings, two prong plates and a lit "|" filament at the muzzle. The chain (scripts/weapons/pipe_arc.gd, one per shooter, made once): every arc a jagged camera-facing ribbon (a kink every 0.4 m, up to 0.17 m off the line, re-jagged 26 times a second, bowed up 5% of its length) with a hot core in a brass sheath, braided with two thinner filaments; no wider on screen than 3% of the eye's distance, so the muzzle's arc is a thread; down the main arc the packets: bright bars across it every 0.55 m flowing at 18 m/s from source to sink, the pipe's "|" (floating "|" glyphs between links' tags were tried and dropped: a lone "|" reads as a pole, and plain daemons die before their tags can frame it); each link lit 0.04 s after the one before, a bloom on it, a spill of data streaks flung on along the stream (a new ParticleFx PIPE pool, 4 x 24; the particle budget 1,600 became 1,700 for it); a synthesised crackle; a PIPE muzzle family. Colour: brass #a79300 (text #b4a019, lightness 0.70; cores and packets #fbecb8): every blue and cyan was taken and the warm half crowded, so a search of the whole colour cube for the farthest point (OKLab) from every bright palette colour, neither hostile red nor any green, with chmod's frost opal and its texts kept 0.16 clear (the first pick, #a88c00, was 0.150 from the frost opal and its text 0.148, under C29's own 0.15 guard), found an olive brass: nearest the signs' sodium 0.151, the frost opal 0.162, the zombie's amber 0.167, rm -rf's ember 0.173; Black ICE's text 0.213, its slate 0.243, glacier 0.286, the hunter's cobalt 0.459, the breakers' ultraviolet 0.428 (its text 0.322), rogue.d's crimson 0.254, the runaway's hot magenta 0.334; the text 0.130 from sodium and amber, 0.178 from the frost opal. Kill word SIGPIPE ("SIGPIPE sent by"), fiction "pipe |: stdout into stdin". Match: protocol 16, no new message and no changed signature (28 RPCs, 32 sends): a chain is the usual fire intent; the host believes it by the usual checks plus the buffer, then _pipe_judge: a joiner's chain built again in the host's world (its ray from the joiner's origin, the pool the host's daemons and ICE and every other player's body: the host's own player, the avatars), every player link landed in the host's combat (an eighth host-only damage site), a joiner's daemon and ICE links landed there while the daemons fight; the host's own chain is the one its player built and drew that frame (pipe_last()), since its own hits already changed the arena (a daemon down is out of the pool). Every screen draws another's chain from the shot message it already gets (_shot's ends: the chain's points in order, at most root_max_links; a miss is its end twice, since no two links are one point). The bump: the loadout grew and _shot's ends mean a chain for the pipe, which an older build can neither believe nor draw. In a match whose daemons do not fight, the host's own idle dummies are in its pool too (their places are every screen's), nothing landed on them. The aim's ray in a match removes the first cron mine on its way, as every shot's does (C27; caught in the self-review, the first build had none). Tests: step 44, tests/pipe_chain_test.gd (37 checks; 38 deliberate breaks, each failing it by a named check with no script error); older pins extended: protocol 16 and "host 16", eleven weapons, the wheel through slot 11, eight host-only damage sites and nine damage_scale sites, pipe_chain.tres beside the tunings and the arsenal's pin with the pipe's lines out, the particle budget 1,700, eleven models and families, the weapon test's beam mode (needs its tuning, a fire mode outside the enum refused). Captures: c30-chain (four daemons, the chain written out), c30-strip (cut from it, 3x), and beyond the brief's two: c30-frozen (through a frozen zombie, shattering), c30-wall (the low wall breaking a chain), c30-ice (a zombie, the frozen hunter, the jam node) and c30-black (Black ICE refusing it). The first stills had thin arcs, glyphs that read as poles and a cone of light from the muzzle; they were redone before the stills were kept.
- C31 as built (ours, 2026-10-29): /dev/null, the black-hole grenade, weapon 12 (bank 2's third: Shift+3; Q and the wheel reach it; the strip "Shift+ 1 2 [3 /dev/null]", bank 2 now showing a thrown weapon's charges as bank 1 does, "3(2)"), PRD 5.2's "/dev/null: black-hole grenade" (scripts/weapons/: dev_null_hole.gd the throw and the hole's rules, dev_null_look.gd the hole as drawn, dev_null_tuning.gd; scripts/hud/dev_null_hud.gd; scripts/viewmodels/dev_null.gd; shaders/dev_null_lens, dev_null_disc, dev_null_debris and dev_null_floor.gdshader; every number in resources/dev_null.tres, the WeaponDef with its DevNullTuning inside it). Picked: thrown on the fork bomb's path (fire_mode PROJECTILE, the DevNullTuning in the projectile slot the fork bomb's ForkBombTuning fills; is_hole()), no quick-throw key (every letter is taken; G stays the fork bomb's); 2 charges, one back every 16 s while under 2 (Inventory and the host's combat alike through the tuning's step_charges(), the host a quarter quicker by the fire-rate tolerance so it is never stricter), a new life full, root free; a throw every 1 s; one hole open at a time (live_cap 1). The throw: 13 m/s along the aim plus 3 up, 15 m/s2, the device opening where it first meets the level or a body, its core 1 m over a floor (never into the level over it; off a wall 0.3 m out), or in the air when its 1.6 s fuse runs out. The hole: opening 0.4 s, pulling 4 s, collapsing 0.35 s; it holds what is within 9 m of its core's vertical axis (11.25 m as root, C22's one more of the weapon's own thing), within 3 m above or below it, and that the core can see (nothing behind the level is pulled, ever): dragged toward the axis at 1 m/s at the edge rising with the square of the depth to 7.5 m/s on it, times a ramp from 35% to full over the first second (and up to 2.5 times as it collapses), swirled round the axis by 55% of that (counter-clockwise from above); a kinematic drag, not a force (no momentum to fight), never into the level (a step that would cross the level drops its swirl, then waits); walkers stay on their floor, the swarm and the hunter are drawn to the core's height too, but never down out of its sight (a flyer over a low wall was first drawn down behind it and let go: caught by the test, fixed). Damage: nothing outside the 3 m horizon; inside it 8 a second at the horizon rising to 40 on the axis, dealt every 0.25 s and quietly (TargetDummy's and IcePiece's quiet flag: no flash, no recoil, no spray, nothing roused; each tick flaring made a knot of daemons a strobing white blob in the first stills); whatever reaches the core (0.8 m) is discarded: one hit of 500, which kills a daemon of any health and is far over chmod 000's 40 (so a frozen one shatters, C29's rule), and ICE's own hit cap for ICE (100, once); then the implosion: everything still held within 5 m of the core takes 45 at the core easing to 10 at 5 m. Daemons: a held daemon does nothing of its own (DaemonBrain._held_by_hole(), chmod's pattern: a tell or a strike called off, "pulled" noted, its attack's wait after; moved, so it walks home in practice); the crowd drawn together is the knot for pipe | or the fork bomb (no rule of their own: the hole puts the daemons in reach). Frozen (C29): pulled like any other, nothing taken on the way in, shattered at the core. Cron mines (C27): any owner's in its reach torn loose and drawn to the core (every screen moves its copy), where one is set off (CronMine.set_off(): one last run there, its own blast and judge, its owner's; the host's run word tells every joiner), its job then over. ICE: the hunter pulled and hurt as a daemon (its discard ICE's 100); the nodes anchored, never moved, hurt; walls never moved or hurt, inside the horizon (and 2 m round it) only flickering, every 0.45 s; Black ICE never pulled, held or hurt, its seals and touch I3's: one flash, and the hole's tag adds "/dev/null: device busy". Decoys, breaker pickups, fork bomblets and slugs are never pulled. The thrower is never pulled or hurt by its own hole. Players (a match): each screen drags its own player from its own copy of the hole (scripts/player.gd _drag_by_holes(): level, at most 4.2 m/s against a run's 7, so running out gets out; never while dead or spawn-protected, never its own hole), the copy opened at the host's place on the host's word; the host judges every player it reaches each tick (the horizon's damage), at the core (a discard of 500: a kill, credited to the thrower, "> /dev/null" in the feed) and at the implosion, never the thrower, a dead or a spawn-protected player (_judge_hole, the ninth host-only damage site; damage_scale for root there and in _spawn_hole's judge of the daemons, eleven sites). In a match whose daemons do not fight, the thrower's own screen pulls and hurts its own idle dummies (pipe |'s rule, C30). Match: protocol 17, no new message (28 RPCs): a throw is the fork bomb's _throw (the host's checks, its count of each player's charges and its live cap of holes), the host's seed on _bomb (its sign the thrower's root: a wider hole), and _blast's generations HOLE_OPENED (the host's core), HOLE_COLLAPSED and HOLE_DISCARDED (the count; the thrower's callout). The look (scripts/weapons/dev_null_look.gd): the device a sphere of black glass in a cage of two plum rings turning, a white-hot pinhole, a ParticleFx trail; the hole a sphere of nothing (opaque, unlit black, 0.75 m: it hides what is behind it and the ring's far side while the ring's near side passes in front), a camera-facing lensing disc 6.8 m across (shaders/dev_null_lens.gdshader: the opaque view behind it bent by a point lens, an Einstein ring at 1.45 times the shadow, the image inverted within it, easing back to the plain view at its edge; a thin white-hot photon ring at the shadow's edge and a turning halo at 1.32 times it), an accretion ring 8.4 m across tilted 22 degrees (dev_null_disc: plum matter on log spirals, orbiting faster inward, white-hot at its inner edge, beaming brighter on the side swinging toward the eye, additive), 150 debris streaks spiralling in from up to 5.6 m (dev_null_debris: a MultiMesh whose paths the vertex shader draws from each instance's custom data, no CPU a frame), the floor's mark (dev_null_floor: a dark well toward the core, the reach a ring of drifting dashes, the horizon a faint line), a plum light (1.3, 7 m) and a negative light at the core (3.0, 4.5 m) that darkens what is near it; what it holds drawn out along the pull up to 2.4 times its length (its volume kept) and tipped 0.45 rad into it (DaemonArt.stretch_toward(), put back when let go), a plum spark trail behind each (a marker per catch that a ParticleFx trail follows); over the core its tag, cron's style, "$ cat * > /dev/null" over "N held  N discarded" (and Black ICE's refusal); the implosion its own node for 1.1 s: a white-hot point popping and dying, a shock ring flung 8 m over the floor and an upright one round the core, the HOLE particles drawn in (a new pool, 2 x 48: the particle budget 1,700 became 1,800), a flash of light, "> /dev/null" rising and fading. Sounds (SfxSynth): a looped low drawn-in roar while it holds, a rush and a deep boom at the implosion, made once and kept. The HUD (scripts/hud/dev_null_hud.gd) only warns a dragged player: the edges washed plum by the drag and "> /dev/null: pulling you in  RUN" 34 px over the crosshair; the thrower reads the hole on the hole (a readout over the crosshair sat on the hole itself in the stills, and the bottom middle is the guide's notices'). The round HUD calls "> /dev/null" at a discard. The strip: "/dev/null  2 left", and while a charge comes back "recharge [#####.....]" (amber with none left). The model (scripts/viewmodels/dev_null.gd): a containment cradle, 1,672 triangles (all twelve 10,568): a dark hex receiver with vents, a ring collar, three curved prongs cupping a sphere of black glass in seven slices, a photon ring hugging it (breathing), two gyroscope rings of plum light on crossed axes turning, inward chevrons down both flanks, a charge cell for each charge, lit by the charges left; its muzzle burst the launchers' family (the fork bomb's), no new family. Colour: the void plum #ab389b (DEV_NULL; HI #ffd9f6 the photon ring and the ring's inner edge; TEXT #ef8fe3, lightness 0.78): every hue band of the bright palette is held, so a search of the plum band between the breakers' ultraviolet and the fork bomb's magenta for the farthest point (OKLab) from every bright colour found 0.159 from chmod's frost opal, 0.160 from the breakers' ultraviolet and the runaway's hot magenta, 0.162 from SIGTERM's violet, 0.178 from the glitch split, 0.186 from cron's rose; ICE's glacier 0.315, the hunter's cobalt 0.283, Black ICE's slate 0.230 and its text 0.316, the pipe's brass 0.312 and its text 0.333; darker colours scored farther only on paper (a glow multiplies them back toward ember and crimson); the text is 0.101 from the breakers' text and 0.103 from chmod's (the band is full; it lives only on the strip). Step 45 holds the plum 0.155 from every bright colour and 0.22 from ICE's, Black ICE's and the pipe's. Kill word "> /dev/null", fiction "> /dev/null: discarded". Cost (measured on a real GPU at 1920x1080): a hole open and drawn 1.84 and 1.67 ms a frame against 1.77 and 1.82 hidden (no measurable GPU time); one step of its hold over four daemons about 300 microseconds on this machine. Tests: step 45, tests/dev_null_test.gd (39 checks; 24 deliberate breaks, each failing it by a named check with no script error); older pins extended: protocol 17 and "host 17", twelve weapons, the wheel through slot 12, bank 2's row with "3(2)", nine host-only damage sites and eleven damage_scale sites, dev_null.tres beside the tunings and the arsenal's pin with it out, the particle budget 1,800, twelve models. Captures: c31-hole (four daemons dragged in), c31-strip (cut from it, 3x), and beyond the brief's one: c31-implosion, c31-knot (pipe | through the knot), c31-ice (the hunter pulled, the gate unmoved) and c31-black ("device busy"). The first stills failed the art bar (a white-pink glow with no black core; the stretch too faint; the knot a white blob; the implosion a white explosion) and were redone before the stills were kept.
- C32 as built (ours, 2026-10-29): SIGHUP, the confusion grenade, weapon 13 (bank 2's fourth: Shift+4; Q and the wheel reach it; the strip "Shift+ 1 2 3(2) [4 SIGHUP]", /dev/null's charges beside it), PRD 5.2's "SIGHUP: confusion grenade; enemies 'reload' and wander" (scripts/weapons/: sighup_burst.gd the throw and the burst's rules, sighup_look.gd the burst as drawn, sighup_tuning.gd; scripts/fx/hup_look.gd a target's confused look; scripts/hud/sighup_hud.gd a caught player's screen; scripts/viewmodels/sighup.gd; shaders/sighup_wave, sighup_curtain and sighup_glitch.gdshader; every number in resources/sighup.tres, the WeaponDef with its SighupTuning inside it). Picked: thrown on the fork bomb's path (fire_mode PROJECTILE, the SighupTuning in the projectile slot; is_hup(); WeaponDef.charges_tuning() names /dev/null's and SIGHUP's alike, so the inventory, the host's combat and the strip count both the same way, DevNullTuning.charges_after() the one rule), no quick-throw key (every letter is taken; G stays the fork bomb's); 2 charges, one back every 12 s, a throw every 0.8 s, two devices in the air at most (live_cap 2). The throw: 14 m/s along the aim plus 3 up, 15 m/s2; it goes off where it first meets the level or a body (its middle 0.5 m over a floor; 0.3 m off a wall or a ceiling), or in the air when its 1.2 s fuse runs out. The burst: a wave running out at 14 m/s to 7 m (8.75 m as root: C22's one more of the weapon's own thing), half a second; each thing in reach is caught as its front passes it, once a burst, and only when nothing of the level lies between the burst's middle and its own (spared otherwise); it does no damage of its own. Daemons (DaemonBrain.hangup()): a confusion beside the brain's State, not one of them (the state word's three bits were full): Hup NONE, RELOAD, WANDER. Caught, a tell or a strike it was in is called off (hup_offs), its attacker slot let go, its prey and its last sight dropped; RELOAD 1.2 s, posed as a recovery (the wire carries RECOVER); WANDER 4 s: legs of 1.1 s (30% either way, its dice seeded by its name and its hangups: the same walk every run) at 55% of its speed, between them 0.6 s looks round (its facing snapping toward random yaws within 1.1 rad of where it stood, every 0.22 s: its visor sweeping), still for 30% of each 5 Hz beat (a stutter), a leg past 80% of its leash turned back toward home; no prey, no lure, no tell on a player; then it is itself again (RETURN after its attack's own wait), guarded 6 s: a hangup while it is confused or guarded is refused, never a restart (at most 5.2 s of every 11.2, 46%). Lashes, where the daemons fight (never in practice, never the hunter): from 0.4 s into the wander, 1.4 s apart, at most 2 a confusion, at the nearest other living daemon within 3.5 m in sight of its eye: its own tell, then its own strike at that daemon (rogue.d's lunge closing the gap and its slash where the scythes come down; the zombie's bolt, a real GlitchBolt whose lash_scale lands on the daemon it meets; the swarm's dive), 75% of the attack's own damage (15, 9, 3.75), landed by the director's hurt_daemon() as a hit with no weapon's name (the hunter adapts to weapons, not to a daemon's claws) and credited to the hangup's thrower (alone this player's; in a match hit_by_other with the thrower's id). A confused daemon is not roused by a hit (no swarm scatter either) and a ping never staggers it (its lash is no attack on a player). Frozen by chmod 000 when caught (C29), it takes the hangup and starts confused when it thaws; a freeze and SIGSTOP hold its confusion's clock, a /dev/null hole does not, and drags a confused daemon 1.3 times as hard (hole_pull_scale: it does not brace, C31). Practice: the dummies reload and wander, never lash, then walk home. A death clears it, and so does a joiner's own screen deciding again (a match left, practice on) while it shows the host's word of one: that confusion was lent, with no numbers of its own, and ends quietly (the self-review's find: it had stepped a null tuning every frame). Its look on a target (scripts/fx/hup_look.gd, made once per target; DaemonArt.hup_phase): over its name tag "SIGHUP: reloading config" typed out with its dots and a bar, then while it wanders a "?" (84 px on screen, bobbing, flickering, now and then "??"); its seams in the carrier green (the daemon shaders' instance slot 7, hup: reloading, its light dimmed under bands of green climbing it; wandering, each part cycling between its own colour and the green and dropping out between); its glitch twitching (0.16 reloading, 0.08 wandering, spikes of 0.4: higher, the magenta split drowned the green in the first stills); its pose held for 80% of each 7 Hz beat while it reloads and 40% while it wanders. ping (C28) outlines a confused daemon in the carrier green, its scan 2.5 times as quick, its tag "<name>  Nm  hung up". ICE: the hunter's brain is a DaemonBrain, confused the same way (never lashing), and IceDirector.forget_learning() wipes the round's ledger, its adaptation and its shields' tint (the HUD's "HUNTER ADAPTED" line gone); a node takes state RELOAD (IceNode.State 4, inside the word's three bits) for 5 s: a tell it was in called off, its dome down, no field, then it looks again, guarded 6 s; frozen when caught, it reloads when it thaws; walls are skipped (never touched, never asked); Black ICE flashes once and the burst's tag adds "SIGHUP: ignored (nohup)" (I3's rules: never confused, held or hurt, its seals and touch untouched). Players (a match): the host's _judge_hup each frame of the wave: each player its front has passed, in reach and sight, once a burst (one spawn-protected when reached is spared for that burst), never the thrower or a dead player; no damage (no damage site); the host's own player confused on its own screen, a joiner told alone (_blast's HUP_PLAYER, through _tell_blast's one-peer filter, so no new send). On the caught player's screen (player.take_hup(): 2.5 s, easing out over its last third): scripts/hud/sighup_hud.gd's glitch (sighup_glitch.gdshader: rows of green static tearing across, scanlines at the edges, its alpha at most 0.55 and the middle only grain), the crosshair as drawn drifting up to 22 px on a slow wander of two rates (crosshair.gd set_drift()) with a faint dot left at the true middle (the aim never moves), "SIGHUP: hung up  NO CARRIER" 58 px over the crosshair (/dev/null's warning has 34), the guide HUD's badge "your AI: HUNG UP" with "SIGHUP   the line hung up, redialling..." (Black ICE's cut wins when both hold). No control is inverted, slowed or taken away (step 46 walks and fires while confused). Match: protocol 18, no new message (28 RPCs): a throw is the fork bomb's _throw (the host's checks and its count of each player's charges), _bomb's seed (its sign the thrower's root), _blast's generations HUP_WENT (where it went off), HUP_CAUGHT (the count confused so far: the thrower's callout and the tag) and HUP_PLAYER (to the one player caught, its id the index); a daemon's Hup rides the daemons' state word in two bits over its life (life_words, DaemonBrain.HUP_SHIFT), the hunter's in the ninth number of ICE's hunter place (IceDirector.HUNTER_WORD), a node's reload is its state. The look (scripts/weapons/sighup_look.gd): the device a telephone handset (an eight-sided handle, two cups, their grilles lit, a carrier light) tumbling at 2.2 turns a second with a ParticleFx trail; the burst a flash of carrier light (3.0 falling away over 0.4 s, and 0.45 riding the wave, its range the front plus 2 m, falloff 1.8), a white-green point popping, a spike of light 4.5 m tall for 0.4 s; on the floor the carrier wave (sighup_wave.gdshader: its front a sine trace of 26 cycles and an overtone running round the ring, a thin edge glow, three ghost traces trailing 0.68, 1.72 and 3.12 m behind it in dashes that drop out, static on an integer hash over the floor it has swept, a 40 degree stretch of the trace torn out now and then); on the front a curtain 2.6 m tall of thin interference lines climbing it, its slices dropping out (sighup_curtain.gdshader: lines only); the HUP particles flung out flat (a new pool, 2 x 48: the particle budget 1,800 became 1,900); its tag 3.6 m over it, drawn over the level ("$ kill -HUP", "NO CARRIER  N reloading", Black ICE's refusal); all of it fading over 1.1 s once the front reaches its edge. Sound (SfxSynth.hangup()): a handset slammed down, a modem's screech flipping between two tones and falling, two beeps of a busy line (480 and 620 Hz). The round HUD calls "kill -HUP  NO CARRIER" at a burst's first catch. The strip: "SIGHUP  2 left", and while a charge comes back "recharge [#####.....]" (amber with none left). The model (scripts/viewmodels/sighup.gd): an old telephone handset in hard geometry, 1,480 triangles (all thirteen 12,048): an eight-sided handle bending down into two cups whose grilles are lit, finger ridges and trim down its flanks, the coiled cord springing out of the mouthpiece, three carrier rings breathing off the earpiece along the aim (dark with no charge left), a carrier light per charge on its spine and a hook switch between them; its muzzle burst the launchers' family. Colour: the carrier green #00bf00 (SIGHUP, an old modem's carrier light; HI #ccffc2 the wave's front and the scan; TEXT #87d35c, lightness 0.79): every hue band of the bright palette is held, so a search of all of sRGB (lightness 0.55 to 0.9, chroma over 0.11) for the farthest point (OKLab) from every bright colour found it here: 0.164 from the pipe's text and 0.170 from its brass, the nearest, 0.176 from the code rain's green and 0.188 from the player's teal; ICE's glacier 0.302, the hunter's cobalt 0.511, Black ICE's slate 0.302, the breakers' ultraviolet 0.530, chmod's frost opal 0.297, /dev/null's plum 0.449, the daemons' crimson 0.423, amber 0.257 and hot magenta 0.487, ping's lime 0.230; the text 0.135 from the pipe's text, the nearest. Step 46 holds the green 0.16 from every bright colour and 0.165 from those named. Kill word "kill -HUP", fiction "SIGHUP: NO CARRIER", skin "carrier". Cost (a real GPU at 1920x1080): a burst held mid-wave and drawn 1.71 and 1.99 ms a frame against 1.69 and 1.89 hidden (no measurable GPU time); one step of its sweep 145 to 292 microseconds across runs on this machine. Tests: step 46, tests/sighup_test.gd (39 checks; 26 deliberate breaks, each failing it by a named check with no script error); older pins extended: protocol 18 and "host 18", thirteen weapons, the wheel through slot 13, bank 2's row with "4(2)", the daemon shaders' slots (hup 7), sighup.tres beside the tunings and the arsenal's pin with it out, the particle budget 1,900, thirteen models. Captures: c32-hup (the burst mid-wave over four daemons), c32-strip (cut from it, 3x), c32-confused (a fighting crowd confused, rogue.d's lash at the zombie mid-strike), c32-player (a caught player's own screen), c32-ice (the hunter and a node reloading, the gate untouched) and c32-black ("SIGHUP: ignored (nohup)"). The first stills failed the art bar (a flat green plate across the floor, the curtain's sides two green slabs, the confused daemons' glitch magenta instead of green, every tag a metre too high) and were redone before the stills were kept.
- C33 as built (ours, 2026-10-29): unlocks, mods and light skins, PRD 5.2's "every one gets its own Tron light skin, mods and unlocks earned by playing" (scripts/unlocks/: progress.gd the save and the earned rule, loadout.gd a player's look, weapon_kit.gd, weapon_skin.gd and weapon_mod.gd the data's scripts; scripts/hud/loadout_screen.gd, loadout_stage.gd its turntable, unlock_toast.gd; each weapon's kit beside its .tres, resources/<id>_kit.tres; the WeaponDef's C16 slots name the kit's ids, skin_slots its own skin first, mod_slots its one mod). Not a weapon: no bank slot, no new key. **Earned:** local and offline only (no store, no money, no account, no network call, no telemetry, nothing bought); a feat counts where this machine judged it with the daemons' director deciding (alone against the C27b daemons fighting back, or a match's host for its own player), never in practice (the director resting), and in a match from the host's word for this player alone (_death naming its weapon; _daemon_struck now naming the weapon of a joiner's hit). Feats: kills with each weapon (the killing hit's source, target_dummy.killed_with), rail_heads (kill -9 headshots), bomb_triples (one fork bomb's tree derezzing 3), pings (daemons a ping met), freezes, shatters (a frozen daemon killed), chains3 (a pipe chain landing 3 links), discards, hups (each caught by SIGHUP), lash_kills (a confused daemon's lash killing another, the thrower's), ice_walls (broken by this machine's own hit), black_ice (a round survived with Black ICE out), grep_marks. **The list** (mod, then the two skins; each skin's nearest hostile light in OKLab): cannon overclock (10 kills: 0.13 s interval, 21 a hit, from 0.16 s and 25), tty1 #33ff33/#c8ffd0 (25 kills; 0.216 from amber), Dracula #bd93f9/#50fa7b (75; 0.161 from glacier, the nearest of all); SIGTERM choke (8 kills: a 4 degree cone, 7 pellets, from 6 and 9), ^C #55ffff/#e0ffff (20; 0.219 glacier), Catppuccin #f5c2e7/#cba6f7 (50; 0.166 glacier); the rail quickdraw (3 kills: a 0.15 s charge, a 3.6 meter, from 0.35 s and 3.0), blacklight #ad33ff/#e0d0ff (3 headshots; 0.174 glitch magenta), P1 phosphor #41ff00/#c8ffd0 (a Black ICE round survived; 0.216 amber); fork bomb ulimit -u (6 kills: 3 bombs, trees of 3 generations, from 2 and 4), C64 #7c70da/#aaffee (15; 0.174 glacier), exponential #ccff00/#f4ffb3 (1 tree of 3 kills; 0.197 amber); fork() deep fork (8 kills: 2 forks, 3 shells, from 1 and 4), jade #2dd5b7/#c2fff0 (20; 0.165 glacier), init #8b59ff/#e0d0ff (50; 0.176 the hunter's cobalt); grep grep -m 4 (15 marks: 4 marks, a 0.8 s lock, from 3 and 0.5 s), --color=always #50fa7b/#e0ffe8 (15 kills; 0.230 amber), regex #ffff55/#ffffcc (40; 0.188 amber); rm -rf no prompt (4 kills: a 0.35 s wind-up, 0.75 of its blast on its shooter, from 0.6 s and 0.5), --preserve-root #e8f0ff/#ffffff (12; 0.238 amber), dd #ccff00/#f4ffb3 (5 ICE walls; 0.197 amber); cron */1 (4 kills: a run every 1.4 s, 2 runs, from 2 s and 3), @midnight #7c70da/#e0d0ff (12; 0.174 glacier), @reboot #33ff66/#c8ffd0 (30; 0.216 amber); ping ping -i 5 (10 found: a 5 s cooldown, a 13 m ring, from 7 s and 18 m), traceroute #55ffff/#e0ffff (30; 0.219 glacier), echo reply #f5c2e7/#ffe6f6 (100; 0.206 amber); chmod 000 deep tank (5 freezes: a 5.5 s tank, a 2.2 s freeze on a daemon, from 4 s and 3 s), chmod 755 #50fa7b/#e0ffe8 (3 shatters; 0.230 amber), chattr +i #e8f0ff/#ffffff (12; 0.238 amber); pipe xargs (3 chains of 3: 6 links, 7 as root, 60% kept a link, from 5, 6 and 70%), tee #2dd5b7/#c2fff0 (15 kills; 0.165 glacier), mkfifo #8b59ff/#e0d0ff (15 chains; 0.176 cobalt); /dev/null wide horizon (2 discards: an 11 m pull, a 3 s hold, from 9 m and 4 s), /dev/zero #f2f2f2/#ffffff (8; 0.226 amber), /dev/random #33ff66/#aaffee (25; 0.245 amber); SIGHUP wideband (6 hung up: a 9 m burst, a 2.8 s wander, from 7 m and 4 s), dial tone #8be9fd/#e0f8ff (1 lash kill; 0.178 glacier), 300 baud #ffff55/#ffffcc (5; 0.188 amber). A first unlock inside a normal session: the cannon's overclock at 10 kills. **Skins:** 26 earned, each a palette name (palette.gd SKIN_* colours, SKINS; two weapons may share a colour, never a skin), a display name and a fiction line from a terminal theme or an old machine; guarded: every earned skin's body and beam at least 0.16 (OKLab) from every hostile light (Palette.HOSTILE_LIGHTS: hostile red, crimson, amber, hot magenta, the glitch's magenta, glacier, the hunter's cobalt, Black ICE's slate; a search of the hue wheel at that floor left the greens, yellow-greens, cyans, violets, pale pinks and whites), a weapon's skins 0.15 apart (its own among them), every beam lightness 0.75 and up (the strip's text). A skin relights the viewmodel (trim, seams, rim, hot parts: viewmodel.gd reads the def's light_skin, the model rebuilt on a change), its muzzle burst, tracers and trails, thrown devices and their bursts and holes (Palette.skin_hot(): a third colour or the beam run 55% to white), chmod's beam and pipe's arcs (paint()), ping's ring (a material per skin), and the strip's drawn entry and line (WeaponHud.skin_tint(): mint in the weapon's own skin); what a weapon leaves on a target keeps the weapon's own colour (frost, the confusion's green, ping's outlines, the hunter's adapted tint), so a state reads the same for everyone. **Mods:** one a weapon (WeaponDef.MOD_SLOTS_LAST 1), gains and costs as numbers by name (a tuning's as "<slot>:<name>"), each checked against WeaponMod.BETTER (a gain the better way, a cost the worse, at least one of each), applied to a copy whose tunings it reaches are copied too, the copy passing the weapon's own problems(); alone it takes effect at once (Inventory.take_state(): nothing refills, a magazine at most its new size, a reload let go). **The save:** user://progress.cfg through scripts/settings/settings_file.gd (its strict scan: plain literals, 4 KB at most; a temporary name renamed over the old file): [meta] version=1, [progress] format=1, [feats] counts, [unlocked] <weapon>__<item>=true, [loadout] skin_<weapon>="<skin>" and mod_<weapon>=true; a missing, damaged or newer file a fresh profile; each bad value left out alone; saved at once on an unlock, a choice or a reset, the counts at each round's end, a pause, quitting and at most every 30 s; headless no path. **The match:** a look is a byte a weapon (the skin's kit index, MOD_BIT 0x10) in slot order: the hello carries the joiner's after its name (LOOK_LAST 18), the host cleans it and clears its mod bits while its "mods" rule (settings multiplayer mods, on by default) is off, fixed when it starts hosting and told on _rules; every roster carries each player's accepted look; combat.gd keeps each player's worn weapons and judges its shots, throws, reloads, charges, coolant, buffer and ult meter with them; every screen draws each player's shots, throws, beams and arcs in its skins (NetSession.def_of()); a joiner wears the host's accepted look from its first roster, and its own choices again when the match ends; a look is fixed for a match. Protocol 19: the hello, the roster, _rules and _daemon_struck changed; no new message (28 RPCs). **The loadout screen** (Esc menu, Loadout, between Resume and Settings): the arsenal (key, name, earned of its kit), the turntable (the model large in a world of its own on a black glass floor that reflects it (SSR), over a small matte plinth ringed in its skin's light, a narrow pool of that light from above, a cool key light and a rim light behind in it, AgX and glow; 0.42 rad/s from each weapon's three-quarter view, tipped 0.2 rad; its size 42% by 54% of the window, 420x300 at least and 860x600 at most; the arena shaded 0.96 behind the screen), the skin cards and the mod card; a card under the pointer lights the model at once (locked too); a click wears or fits a held one; a locked one "LOCKED  n / m <feat>" with a bar; up and down pick the weapon; Esc back; alone what is worn changes as it closes. **The toast:** "UNLOCKED: <name>" 92 px from the top, 460x70 (scaled with a window taller than 720 px, up to 1.6x), its corners brackets of the item's light, a sweep, 3.4 s, several in the order reached. **Reset progress:** the settings' footer, a second click within 4 s. **--unlock-all:** everything held for the run, never saved. The nixfred logo never touched (the test pins docs/media/nixfred-icon.svg). Captures: c33-loadout (the cannon in Dracula, its overclock fitted), c33-locked (a locked dial tone lighting the SIGHUP handset), c33-skins (six weapons in six earned skins on an arc) and c33-unlock (the toast over a SIGTERM shot in ^C); the loadout screen costs no measurable GPU time (1.48 and 1.97 ms a frame open, 1.79 and 1.58 closed, 1920x1080, a real GPU). Tests: step 47, tests/unlocks_test.gd (49 checks, every feat a kit names checked as counted somewhere); older pins extended (protocol 19 in 21 tests, the hello's look, _rules, _roster and _daemon_struck's signatures, the weapon test's slots, the graphics test reading C33's slot lines back empty and its file list with the kits).

- C38 as built (ours, 2026-10-30): the opener's first 35 seconds (PRD 5.7, 0:00 to 0:35) on demo data, built so C39 to C42 plug in. The data interface: OpenerData (scripts/opener/opener_data.gd), read once as the opener starts: kind(), user(), host(), palette(), folders() (paths under the home folder), uptime_s() (as it starts; the opener ticks it on its own clock), file_times() and now() (local wall-clock seconds: unix time plus the zone's offset), and clean(), which drops escape sequences and control characters, turns tabs and line breaks into spaces, trims, and cuts names at 32 (user, host) or 60 (a folder) with "...". C38 ships only DemoOpenerData, fixed and invented: user "user", host "workstation", 28 folders (the XDG ones, dotfiles, notes, code/ and four projects under it, one with node_modules for C40's pit, taxes-2024, resume-final-v3 and the like), up 3 days 07:42:15, now Tuesday 2025-11-04 22:17, file times with 03:12 that night; nothing real is read (no file, folder, environment, clock, process or theme). The menu and --opener always hand it demo data; an empty source falls back to the demo's names and the default palette. Palettes: OpenerPalette, Omarchy's colors.toml keys as "#rrggbb" (accent, selection, muted, the four backgrounds, the four foregrounds, the eight colours and six bright ones) with a fallback chain ending at the background or the foreground; six demo palettes in resources/opener/ taking their colours from open themes Omarchy ships: night (Tokyo Night, the default), mocha (Catppuccin Mocha), ember (Gruvbox Material), frost (Nord), forest (Everforest), latte (Catppuccin Latte, light). Drawn as Omarchy's terminal does: the cursor in bright_foreground, the border in the accent, the desktop in darker_background, the prompt bash's (user@host bold green, ~ bold blue), folders blue, the uptime yellow. The terminal: Omarchy's tiled window (10 px gaps, a 2 px border, square corners, 14 px padding at 1080 lines, scaled with the height), 30 rows at a 1.2 line spacing at any size (a 14 px font at 960x540, 28 px at 1080), the system's JetBrainsMono Nerd Font, JetBrains Mono, DejaVu Sans Mono, Liberation Mono or monospace (nothing bundled, so no font licence to carry), each character placed in its own cell; a block cursor, solid 0.5 s after a key, then 0.53 s on and off; a 0.18 s pop-in from 92%. The crash: the game's own last frame taken from the viewport (in memory only, never saved, let go at the cut; a near-black stand-in headless), the arena paused and its 3D view not drawn under it from then on, the stuck-buffer buzz; it comes apart from 1.1 to 2.9 s (shaders/opener_glitch.gdshader: slipped slices, a smeared stretch of rows, runs of 32 px blocks stuck on one colour, split channels, bands corrupted toward the daemons' magenta, the colour depth falling), its pattern changing 10 times a second; drops to black at 1.6 and 2.3 s (0.05 s each), cut to black and silence at 3.1 s. Flash safety is a model, luma_at(), that the drawing reads (the frame's darkness, the terminal's pop-in): at most 2 flashes in any second. Timings: freeze 0.4 s; hum 5.6 s; terminal 6.6 s; the prompt types itself from 8.6 s (0.15 s a key, done by 13.4 s at the latest, about 11.1 s with the demo's names); "thanks for running me." on the prompt's own line from 15.2 s, the rest on bare lines with no shell answering: the install line from 18.4 s (a typo, "isn" taken back to "i", and a 0.75 s beat before " did you?"), the folders 23.8 to 25.8 s (speeding up), the uptime at 26.2 s, "I've been in here since you booted." from 27.4 s (a 0.55 s beat before " since"), the late-night line from 31.2 s to 34.6 s at the latest (a 0.8 s beat before its echo), the cursor on its own line at 34.8 s, a band of rows torn for 0.07 s at 35.4 s, the hold to 38 s, then the menu. Lines type at 0.074 s a key on average with seeded jitter and are squeezed to fit their window if the data is long. The late-night line's words are ours (the PRD gives only its source): the latest file time in the past week from 00:00 to 04:59, "you were still up at 03:12 last night." (or "on tuesday" when older than yesterday) then " so was I."; with none late, "you're always in bed by midnight. I don't sleep."; with no times, "I don't sleep. do you?". - C39 as built (ours, 2026-10-30): the opener's real data (PRD 5.7, "built from the player's real machine"): RealOpenerData (scripts/opener/real_opener_data.gd) behind C38's OpenerData seam, opt-in and read-only. The setting: Settings, Privacy and AI, "opener data", kept as [privacy] opener_real_data="on" or "off" in user://settings.cfg by scripts/boot/guide_settings.gd (missing or damaged reads off: the player opts in), with the one-line note "names, counts, times, theme, uptime; no contents" under it and the full sentence as its tooltip; it counts the next time the opener plays; a demo run (the guide link's demo, that is --demo or real-machine data off, both checked) keeps the demo data and the row says "this run: demo data"; with no guide link the menu plays the demo; the first-launch question stays C42's. What it reads, and nothing else: USER (else LOGNAME, else the home folder's own name) and HOME, its only environment (GDScript has no call for either); the host name, the first line of /proc/sys/kernel/hostname (else /etc/hostname), at most 64 bytes; the uptime, the first number in /proc/uptime, at most 64 bytes; the active Omarchy theme's colors.toml (Omarchy 4's ~/.local/state/omarchy/current/theme/, else Omarchy 3's ~/.config/omarchy/current/theme/), at most 16 KB, its #rrggbb colours and mode only, and whether a light.mode file stands beside it; folder names under the home folder, the entries each holds, and modification times; the system clock and its zone's offset ("now", and file times read as local hours). Those three small files are the only ones it opens, in one function, to read; it writes, saves, prints, logs, sends and runs nothing. Its engine calls, each for a reason: OS.get_environment; OS.get_thread_caller_id and get_main_thread_id (its report); Time.get_ticks_usec (the budget), get_unix_time_from_system and get_time_zone_from_system; FileAccess.open (READ), file_exists and get_modified_time; DirAccess.open and dir_exists_absolute; Engine.get_main_loop (joining its thread); on a listed folder only include_hidden, include_navigational, list_dir_begin, get_next, is_link, current_is_dir, list_dir_end and get_filesystem_type. The walk: breadth first, in name order without case: the home folder's own entries, hidden ones included (listed, never walked), then each folder's down to 3 levels, hidden ones below the top skipped; caps of 600 entries, 64 folders listed, 200 entries a folder and 150 ms for the whole read, keeping 128 folder names and the 256 newest times; every node_modules counted (C40's pit) and never walked; a symbolic link never listed, walked or timed (stricter than "not followed out of home"); a folder on another filesystem than the home folder's (DirAccess.get_filesystem_type: a network share, a removable disk) stays a name with no count; a folder it may not read stays a name, quietly; file times only from what is not hidden (an app's cache stirring at 3 a.m. says nothing about the player); handed over in name order, each folder just before what is in it, the hidden top-level ones last (first, a real home's dotfolders filled the scroll); counts, not byte sizes (a size is a stat of everything under a folder, past any cap). Off the main thread: a Thread of its own at low priority (never the worker pool, whose shutdown would wait on a stalled read), each value published under one mutex as it is read; the opener starts it as it starts and takes the values as soon as all are in, or at the tuning's new take_by (3.1 s, the cut, so it never shows; problems() refuses one after terminal_at) regardless; settle(), as the opener takes the values, stops a read still going so nothing changes after (and the folders with their counts, and the file times with "now", are each published under one lock, so the opener never reads one of a pair without the other); drop(), as the opener ends however it ends, cancels a read still going and clears every value, and a thread still running is joined once it ends. Fallbacks, value by value: the user, the host, the uptime, the palette (null: the default), the folders with their counts, and the file times with "now" (together); any one not in by take_by, unreadable or unusable (no theme, a theme under 4.5:1, nothing found) is the demo's alone. The theme: scripts/opener/theme_colors.gd (ThemeColors), by Omarchy's own cascade (omarchy-theme-color): the canonical names, then bg, fg and the other short names, color0 to color15 and purple; dark_background 25% and darker_background 50% toward black, each bright colour 20% toward white, orange from yellow; the mode from mode, theme_type, a light.mode file or a background over 382 of 765; null for a foreground under 4.5:1 on its background or no background or foreground. Readable everywhere: OpenerPalette.readable() pulls green, blue, yellow or bright_foreground under 3:1 on the background toward the foreground, 5% a step, for every palette the opener draws, the demo ones included (latte's green and yellow). Wide characters: scripts/opener/cell_width.gd (CellWidth), a compact approximation of Unicode's East Asian Width and emoji ranges (2 for CJK, kana, hangul, fullwidth forms and emoji; 0 for combining marks, variation selectors, skin tones, a joiner and what it joins, a flag's second letter); FakeTerminal lays rows out in cells (a wide character that would split at a row's end starts the next row), draws a plain character with draw_char as before and a cluster with draw_string, centred and scaled to fit its cells, and back() takes a whole cluster; clean() measures NAME_LONGEST (32) and FOLDER_LONGEST (60) in cells, cuts only between clusters, and drops bidirectional overrides and isolates, the direction marks, word joiners, a zero-width space, the byte-order mark, the soft hyphen and the annotation marks. OpenerData gained start(), is_ready(), settle(), drop() and folder_counts() (DemoOpenerData's invented, none over 200). Step 49, tests/opener_real_test.gd, 46 checks, on a fake home under user:// only.
- C40 as built (ours, 2026-10-30): the opener's shrink-away reveal and directory-tree fall (PRD 5.7, 0:35 to 1:10), on the values the opener already took (the demo's, or C39's reader's with the player's yes); nothing new is read. The beats (OpenerTuning's new Shrink-away and Fall groups): the hold to 36 s; a fresh prompt at once on the cursor's line, as a shell gives one, the terminal ignoring typing for ignore_s, 3 s (every key and click swallowed, as ever); "you're in now." (the PRD's words) typed at that prompt, 39.0 to 40.2 s; at 40.7 s (LIFT_S before shrink_at) the terminal and its screen are moved into a SubViewport of their own, the window's size, its picture shown where they were (pixel for pixel: measured 0.00 of 255), and at 40.8 s the machine's view takes over; the fall at 50 s, the landing at 68.4 s, the hand-off at 69.6 s (C41's: Opener.fall().landing() is the camera at rest, fall() and layout() the world and the city, the machine going on drawing); until C41 the screen fades to black from the hand-off and the opener ends at 70.2 s. problems() refuses an ignore other than 3 s (0.25 either way), "you're in now." before the 3 s are up, the shrink-away outside 0:35 to 0:50, a reveal under 6 s, a fall starting more than a second off 0:50, or landing after 1:10. The machine: TreeFall (scripts/opener/tree_fall.gd), a SubViewport with a world of its own (never the arena's; the main viewport's 3D stays off), 4x MSAA as the loadout stage, a linear tonemap (so the screen's first frame is the terminal's colours exactly), exponential fog with aerial perspective into a sky shader (near black above, red-violet below), glow (screen blend) only with the player's bloom setting; built at the cut (3.1 s, the screen black) and drawn once there to warm its pipelines; the terminal's viewport stops drawing as the fall starts. The reveal: the screen a quad the window's shape, 4.5 m tall, with a 0.14 m bezel in the accent, on the home folder's monolith; the camera starts screen_distance() (4.825 m) square in front of it at a 50 degree field of view, where it fills the view exactly (the panel shader fetches texels 1:1 there, and averages a 4 by 4 footprint once a texel is smaller than a pixel, so the shrinking text never sparkles), then pulls back 160 m across the shaft (ease in and out, power 2.4, over 80% of the reveal), rising 20 m, the field of view widening to 61, a 7 m sway and 5 degrees of yaw and 1.5 of roll growing with the pull-back, and tips 78 degrees down over the last 40%. The fall: a drop table (512 samples) from a speed shape (picking up over 30% of the fall as a body does, a little more all the way down, braking over the last 17%; 37 m/s at most), a turn table quickest early (198 degrees round the axis in all, so it looks round the top terrace while still among it), the field of view widening to 76, the camera facing across the shaft: its pitch from -78 up to -12 (the top terrace's names at eye level), -30 across, a look down at the pit (-54) between 50 and 80% of the way, -7 landed; a small shake with the speed; a 0.55 m dip on landing; the mouse turns the view at most 0.24 and 0.16 rad, springing back (look only). The city: TreeLayout (scripts/opener/tree_layout.gd), plain numbers seeded by an FNV-1a hash of the names: terraces of radius 150, 112, 82 and 58 m at -70, -190, -300 and -395 m, one a level of the tree (deeper paths on the last), the pit's rim at -470 m (radius 30, 640 m deep, a smaller well inside it), the monolith (72 m wide, 194 m tall) on the top rim toward -Z, the screen on it at 20 m and `~/` over it; each top-level folder a slice of the circle (outside the monolith's) as wide as one plus what it holds (each child counting at most 12), the visible folders flanking the monolith and the hidden ones opposite it, the folders in it on the next terrace in its slice, a cable of light from its rim to each; a tower's width 9 to 22 m and height 24 to 88% of its terrace's tallest by log(1 + entries)/log(201), its windows 10 to 62% lit; not counted (-1, or no count): 11 m wide, 30% as tall, 3% lit; hidden: 0.7 the size, in the back row; one to three setback boxes, a spire now and then, never up through the terrace above or the ceiling; the first node_modules is the pit (no tower: its name over the pit and a magenta cable down into it), any other a maze tower; a name is the folder's base name cleaned to 24 cells plus "/", over its roof, every third a step higher so neighbours seen edge-on never run together, sized by terrace; up to 134 towers with no name in the rows behind (fewer beside the monolith), 40 megastructures beyond, 28 streams of code light from the ceiling down to the terrace under each or into the pit, kept off the camera's way. Caps: the first 512 folders looked at, 96 towers (the shallow ones first), 40 names, 1024 boxes, 96 cables, 28 streams; at most 64 nodes in the machine. The hottest process: OpenerData.top_process(), "" from every source but the demo ("node", invented): nothing reads processes (C39's reader reads none and C40 added no reads). Eight shaders: opener_tower, opener_panel, opener_terrace, opener_maze (the "10 PRINT" pattern), opener_stream, opener_speed, opener_sky, opener_core. Sounds made in scripts/sfx_synth.gd: opener_whoosh (the shrink), opener_wind (a seamless loop, its volume and pitch with the speed), opener_thud (the landing), opener_beat (the process, from 45% of the fall). Flash: the screen's brightness model dims from 1 to 0.3 over the first half of the reveal and fades to black from the hand-off; the process swells once every 1/0.85 s. - T1 as built (ours, 2026-10-31): the teaser's raw footage, a tool and one render (docs/TRAILER.md). tools/trailer.gd is a SceneTree script with a fixed shot list (29 shots, about 87 s in the running order: the opener in six clips, the arena in two, the daemons in four, ICE and Black ICE, all 13 weapons, a wide fight, the title); one run plays one shot (-- --demo --shot=<id>) on the shipped game, driven as the tests and the stills tool drive it (the player's scripted input and aim, daemons placed and turned loose, ICE and Black ICE summoned, the opener opened from the Esc menu), and prints its roll and cut as the movie's frame numbers (checked: the frame drawn at the mark is the movie's frame of that index). tools/trailer_capture.sh renders from a git archive copy of a commit (HEAD by default) on a private Xvnc display (no TCP, a unix socket only the owner can open, a fresh cookie, an empty private XDG_RUNTIME_DIR, no Wayland socket), one Godot run a shot under Movie Maker (--write-movie as Motion JPEG at 0.95 and PCM, --fixed-fps 60; the copy's override.cfg sets the 1920x1080 base size, which Movie Maker records), fresh empty user data a take; it cuts each clip at its marks with ffmpeg (H.264 CRF 14, AAC 320k, 48 kHz), fails a clip that is not 1920x1080 at 60 fps with sound, finds its loudest moments from its own sound (per-frame peaks, the top six 0.3 s apart, louder than -30 dBFS, matched to the sounds the take logged as starting) and writes shots.json (running order, start, end, length, line, loudest, kills, commit). Demo data only, enforced: no --demo, the opener's real data on in its user data, or headless: refused (exit 3); a demo guide link with its own privacy file, real data written off; the opener checked to be on DemoOpenerData before a frame of it is kept. Its own: the camera (smooth paths, never a static frame), the HUD and the daemons' name tags hidden, the 3D at 2x the window's resolution (scaling_3d_scale 2.0, filtered down), extra daemons from fixed seeds, the player's health topped up below 60, a slow push-in on the opener's terminal layer, /dev/null's hold cut short once its crowd is in, the title card (the SIGKILL wordmark in the arena's sign font as neon voxels slamming together, the brand hexagon rendered from the brand file as it is). tests/trailer_test.gd is check step 51 (18 checks).
