- Carlos Granier community prompt (Fred, 2026-10-30 11:27 PM ET, "We have a new commentor to add to the PRD and the credits. Process and add"; public reply on X by Carlos Granier, @cgranier, credited as the third community contributor after @QuantaCortex and @TimProvencio): five ideas go into the PRD as post-1.0 (1.0 scope is locked, 2026-10-29) unless Fred pulls one in. Every one is fully simulated in-game; nothing reads or touches the real system. (1) man sigkill: a training level where each weapon unlocks after you read its man page in-world; the EXAMPLES section is a 20-second drill against a dummy, and --help on any weapon replays its drill. (2) The OOM killer: a boss that wakes under high (in-game) memory pressure and hunts whatever uses the most memory, often you if you keep a big weapon out or hoard ammo; switching to light weapons shrinks you and drops its aggro, pushing use of all 13 weapons. (3) Arenas: /tmp wipes itself every round (cover, ICE walls and cron mines reset); /proc has walls that are live in-game counters you can shoot to change the arena, like freezing a gravity value (game state only, never the real /proc). (4) Uptime is your score for solo runs: each round survived adds uptime, daemons scale with the in-game load average, a kernel panic ends the run with a panic screen showing your stats as a stack trace, and a leaderboard by uptime (local first; any online board is public and waits on Fred). (5) Music generated from the in-game load average: tempo follows how many daemons are alive and the bass drops when Black ICE arrives (original procedural audio, nothing ripped). Credit: CREDITS.md, the README community section and the C44 credits roll name "Carlos Granier (@cgranier)" for these ideas.
- Jeremy community prompt (Fred, 2026-10-30 11:31 PM ET, "2 more!"; public reply on X by Jeremy, @ocdjeremy, credited as the fourth community contributor): post-1.0 unless Fred pulls one in. (1) A toggleable AR overlay in the visor styled as a live htop pane, showing in-game daemons, ICE and players only, never real processes (the real-data Audit Trail visor stays C43, credited to @QuantaCortex). (2) Scoped weapons (grep seeker, kill -9 rail) get stepped zoom levels instead of ADS on or off, with hold vs toggle ADS as a setting (the existing aim hold/toggle setting extends to it). (3) Real hitboxes: headshots and center mass hit hardest, limbs least, and nobody gets one-shot in the toe. (4) Balance each weapon on its own strengths instead of auto-nerfing anything that fires fast. Stretch: VR support (post-1.0, likely 2.x; OpenXR in Godot). Credit: "Jeremy (@ocdjeremy)" in CREDITS.md, the README community section and the C44 credits roll.
- MindGameLabs community prompt (Fred, 2026-10-30 11:31 PM ET, "2 more!"; public reply on X by MindGameLabs, @MindGamesLabs, credited as the fifth community contributor): post-1.0 unless Fred pulls it in. A sudo grenade that will not throw until you type a password, and three wrong tries makes it go off in your hand. Safety: the password is an in-game one shown on screen as a typing challenge, never the player's real password; nothing typed is stored, logged, sent or checked against the system, and no Ctrl+Alt keys are used. It is a new throwable separate from the existing Z sudo overcharge. Credit: "MindGameLabs (@MindGamesLabs)" in CREDITS.md, the README community section and the C44 credits roll.

- Packet tracer arena: tailnet machines as islands

- 2026-10-28 1:22 AM ET: C19 fix-it: the fork bomb test (check step 23, tests/fork_bomb_test.gd) made deterministic; game code unchanged. Root cause, proven by pinning the seeds in a scratch copy and sweeping them (each seed failed the same checks every time, even under parallel load): the game draws a new scatter seed for every throw, and the test's geometry leaned on where the random children landed. The host's thrown bomb went off 4.29 m from the joiner (outside the 4 m blast), so the joiner was hurt, or killed from 10, only when a child flew back toward it; and the test's 4 m wall let a chain spread around its edge. Fix: each case pins its seed source; the joiner stands where the thrown bomb's own blast, whose place no seed moves, takes 13 (2.3 m off); the kill case runs before the self-damage case that may kill the joiner; the wall is 14 m wide, more than a chain spreads (5.4 m each side at most); the host must have heard the joiner's newest place and look before it judges, and each forged throw is waited for until the host counts it, instead of fixed frame counts. All 37 checks kept, none weakened. Proof: 48 seed sets swept, 48/48 pass; 10 solo runs in a row, 10/10 pass with identical output but the random ENet peer id; three full gates, the fork bomb step ok in all three: the first exited 1 on the old cannon flake (fixed since, next line), the next two passed, 23 of 23.
  - Known issue (low): the gate throws the same nine pinned chains every run; other seeds were covered once, by the 48-set sweep, not by the gate.
  - Known issue (low): in the self-damage case the rest of the chain usually kills the joiner (100 -> 0); the check needs only that it is hurt while the thrower is not, so it does not show a partial hit.
  - Known issue (low): the test's waits on host events have wall-clock watchdogs (4 to 8 s); a machine slowed far enough could time one out. None has.
- 2026-10-28 2:01 AM ET: cannon flake fixed (check step 5, tests/cannon_test.gd, "fire and hit sounds both started", failing in 2 of 4 gates); game code unchanged. Root cause, probed in a scratch copy: headless, the Dummy audio driver mixes in steps of 4096 frames, about 93 ms, and the cannon's hit blip (0.09 s) is shorter than one step, so the first mix after the shot uses it up whole. The check read playing 1.5 to 3 ms after the shot, and failed whenever a mix fell in that window (the failing run: fire playing, hit not, 3.0 ms after). Not a game bug: both sounds start, and a real driver's buffer is far smaller. Fix: the test holds the mixer still (AudioServer.lock()) from just before the shot frame until both are read, then unlocks; same check, 46 checks before and after. Proof: with 60 ms of real time added after the shot, the old test failed 11 of 16 runs and the fixed one 0 of 16; with either play() call removed the check still fails; 20 solo runs and 24 runs 8 at a time all exit 0; three full gates exit 0, 23 of 23.

  - Known issue (medium, A2's, found in A3, not fixed here: enemy art is out of A3's scope): daemon_shell and daemon_core declare the instance uniforms clock and hit at different indices, and Godot warns "More than one material in instance export the same instance shader uniform ... The same warnings show at b38a900 (24 in its enemy art test). Fix: pin the indices with instance_index hints, or declare them first and in the same order in both shaders. - 2026-10-29 1:17 PM ET: C31 fix-it, before the weapon: the load flake in step 43 (tests/freeze_ray_test.gd, "the host's beam freezes the joiner, its own meter told back to it by the host", which read the joiner's screen as "" once in the producer's gate at load 7.8) and net_test's 1000 ms delay buffer (the known flake since C13) have one cause, found and proven: ENet's own packet throttle. ENet backs off a peer's unreliable packets when its measured round trip rises; the checks run a host and its joiners in one process over 127.0.0.1, so that round trip is only the shared frame loop's jitter, and on a busy machine ENet dropped the position and meter words (a probe at load 13: the joiner's throttle at 26 of 32, 43 of 50 position words arriving, the buffer 19 deep, failing three runs of three). With ENet's back-off made harsher in scratch copies, step 43 failed by the same check with the same text in 4 of 12 runs (the joiner's own meter, sent back to it on unreliable words, never arrived inside the wait) and nothing else failed. The fix is in the game, one rule for every check at once rather than one in each of 40 joins: a link to this same machine never throttles (scripts/net/net_session.gd: _steady_loopback() on both ends as a link connects, is_loopback() for 127.x.x.x, ::1 and 127.x.x.x mapped into IPv6; ENet tells the other end too), and every other link (a LAN, the internet, a relay elsewhere) keeps ENet's own back-off. After it, net_test passed three runs of three at the same load (21 deep). net_test pins it (a new check: no back-off either way on the joiner's link, only loopback addresses counting); three deliberate breaks (is_loopback() always false, ENet's default back-off kept, a LAN address counted as loopback) each fail that check by name with no script error, and the first two bring the long-delay failure back under load. Step 43's check gained its detail (when the joiner froze, whether it is frozen now, the edge wash, the breaker refusal), unchanged in what it proves. C30's note that the delay buffer counted "game time against a real-time window" was wrong: every clock in the net code is game time; the drops were ENet's.
  - Known issue (low): over a real network ENet's back-off still drops unreliable words when a link's round trip climbs, as designed; a joiner's own freeze meter rides them (C29), so on a bad link a joiner can see its freeze late while the host's judgement holds.

  - Known issue (low): cell widths approximate Unicode's tables rather than carry them whole; an Indic vowel sign takes a cell of its own.
  - Known issue (low): file times are read in the zone's current offset, so one from across a daylight-saving change reads an hour off.
  - Known issue (low): a cold read of a large home can stop on its 150 ms cap after a few hundred entries; what it found by then is what scrolls.
  - Known issue (low, not seen): the opener has never been watched showing a real machine's names (captures stay on demo data by rule; the reader ran on a real home only to print numbers).
